| Fix | Pillar-2 retreat: (1) SSOT → revert to 32-patch state with 4 new wall_adiabatic entries (`APU_door`, `Plane_Outer_Surf`, `Frame_3`, `Frame_6`); (2) each new entry's `role` field explicitly notes "v3/v4 hybrid 曾计划 drop/merge · 实际 STL 仍含此 body · 2026-05-12 audit 退回"; (3) top comment rewritten from "v3 surgery 后 32→30" to "当前 32-patch · 历史 v3/v4 forward-write 未实施 · 未来 surgery 三步路径" |
| Status | confirmed — APU bay naming.yaml retreated 2026-05-12 audit; `make 03_validate` now `[ok] 全部 32 个 patch 在 STL 和 naming.yaml 中一致` |
| Reference case | APU bay `inputs/naming.yaml` 2026-05-12 audit |
| Lesson | **Forward-writing a SSOT to describe planned-but-unimplemented state creates time-skew between documentation and reality.** When the next engineer (or fresh Claude Code session) reads the SSOT, they have no signal that the surgery was planned-but-deferred — the doc reads as describing current state. **Mitigation patterns**: (a) when forward-writing intent, use explicit future-tense markers (`planned`, `TODO`, `pending implementation`); (b) when retreating from forward-write, narrate the retreat in the comment chain so future readers understand the history; (c) where possible, gate forward-writes on actual implementation landing (CI check linking SSOT entries to code paths). **Inheritance**: any project documenting "planned change → SSOT updated → implementation deferred" inherits this trap. **Counter row**: autonomous_governance: true (in-session retreat, no DEC needed for SSOT bookkeeping) |

### V75 · Refinement-level coarsening fixes face count but not max-skewness ceiling — patch identity contradiction is upstream

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | 2026-05-12 audit follow-up: APU bay case_002a v27 mesh failed checkMesh with `max_skewness = 7.999 / 1119 highly skew faces` + 5 failed checks. Per-patch skew localization (`scripts/skew_locate.py`-style probe via direct polyMesh read) revealed **95.4% (1068/1119) of skew faces concentrated on a single patch: `APU_door`**, which v26 (2026-05-10) had newly added as a "physical bay-air inlet" (replacing aliasing to farfield_cylinder) with refinement level (3,4) = 5mm cells. Engineer hypothesis: snap-stress on complex curved door at 5mm cells. F1 fix: coarsen APU_door (3,4) → (2,3) = 10mm. v28 rebuild result: skew face count **1119 → 570 (−49%)**, duplicate faces **101 → 22 (−78%)**, non-consecutive shared points **1888 → 731 (−61%)**, nonManifoldPoints **1467 → 552 (−62%)**, concave cells **180725 → 123711 (−32%)**, total cell count **3.42M → 2.60M (−24%)**. Topology + count dramatically improved; **max_skewness pegged at 7.9991 (essentially unchanged from 7.9994)**. Mesh still FAILS 3 checks. Re-localization: **APU_door still holds 90.2% (514/570) of remaining skew faces** |
| Engineer symptom | F1 looks like a "real fix" by face-count metrics (49-78% improvement across topology counters) but the verdict-gate metric (`max_skewness < 4` for PASS, `< 8` for hard-fail) is unchanged. Engineer reading just the report sees "still FAIL" and may conclude F1 didn't work, when in fact the underlying geometry-quality problem moved from "1068 bad cells on APU_door" to "514 bad cells on APU_door" — same root cause, half magnitude. The 8.0 wall is OpenFOAM's hard rejection ceiling, not a softer continuous metric |
| Root cause | **Patch identity contradiction upstream of mesh quality.** APU_door is a single STL body but has **two inconsistent type assignments**: (a) `inputs/naming.yaml` says `wall_adiabatic`, role "外蒙皮底部舱门 (outer skin bottom door)" — a closed maintenance door; (b) v26 hand-edited `case/system/snappyHexMeshDict` says `patchInfo { type patch; }` with comment "physical bay-air inlet (was missing — farfield was acting as substitute)" — an open flow boundary. Additionally APU_door physically overlaps with `body_1` ("风门 APU intake louver, mojibake"-named body) — two interpretations of the same physical region. sHM tries to snap both to their respective surface meshes, producing sliver cells at the intersection. Refinement level (the F1 lever) controls cell size but cannot eliminate slivers — only geometry surgery (drop one of the overlapping bodies OR explicitly merge/Boolean) can |
| Fix | **F1 (landed 2026-05-12)**: `case/system/snappyHexMeshDict` line 116 `APU_door level (3 4) → (2 3)`; `config/case.yaml` refinement section gained explicit `APU_door: [2, 3]` entry. Result v28: 49% skew face reduction, still FAIL on max-skewness. **F2 attempt (landed 2026-05-12)**: dropped APU_door from both geometry block + refinementSurfaces in snappyHexMeshDict (user picked F2 per "进气口是对的, 但 v26 实现失败 → 先回退"). v29 rebuild result revealed a **second trap**: sHM auto-created a default-named patch `apu_geom_<bodyname>` for the unlisted STL body — 62.4% of v29's 149 skew faces ended up on `apu_geom_APU_door`. Dropping the patch from sHM dict ≠ dropping the body from the mesh. **F2.1 (true revert · landed 2026-05-12)**: STL surgery — used a stream-based regex `re.match('solid APU_door' / 'endsolid APU_door')` to strip the entire APU_door solid block (105,002 lines, 3.6 MB) from `cleaned_combined.stl` (304 MB → 290 MB, 28/29 bodies retained). v30 rebuild result: **max_skewness FINALLY broke 8.0 wall: 7.6239** (vs 7.997 v29, 7.999 v28, 7.999 v27). Skew face count **56** (vs 149 v29, 570 v28, 1119 v27). Topology nearly clean: 8 duplicate / 8 non-consec / 57 nonManifoldPoints (close to v24 baseline of 7+7+0). But still **FAIL 3 checks** — diffuse residue: Outer_Surf 32 (57%), body_4 7 (12.5%), firewall_front 4 (7%), tail. Verdict still > 4 PASS threshold; the v24-class diffuse pattern persists. **F3 (further deferred)**: bring max_skewness < 4 would require Outer_Surf STL refinement / repair OR relaxed `meshQualityControls`; out of scope for this session |
| Status | F1 + F2 + F2.1 all landed; **mesh still FAIL but 8.0 wall broken and topology cleaned**. APU_door body fully removed from STL + dict + SSOT; bay-air inlet temporarily re-aliased to farfield_cylinder (pre-v26 state); future re-introduction of APU_door as `mass_flow_inlet` requires body_1 louver geometry reconciliation in dedicated session |
| Reference case | APU bay case_002a · v27 → v28 → v29 → v30 mesh iteration arc · 2026-05-12 |
| Lesson | **Three independent levers, three independent ceilings**: (1) refinement level (F1) controls cell count + topology counters but cannot break the 8.0 max-skewness wall when a single patch holds 90%+ of skew faces; (2) sHM dict edits (F2) drop named patches but DO NOT drop the underlying STL body — sHM auto-creates `<geom-name>_<body-name>` default patches, and 62% of v29 skew still went there; (3) **STL surgery (F2.1) is the only intervention that actually drops a body from the mesh** — F2.1's 87% skew face reduction (149 → 56) and the breaking of the 8.0 wall (7.997 → 7.624) are direct empirical proof. **Diagnostic pattern**: when checkMesh produces concentrated skew on a single patch, run per-patch skew localization BEFORE tuning sHM controls. If ≥80% of skew faces are on one patch, the fix is geometry (STL or solid block surgery), not sHM parameters. **Anti-pattern**: chasing skewness via `nSolveIter` / `nSmoothPatch` / `meshQualityControls.maxNonOrtho relaxed` when the source is overlapping STL bodies — relaxation lets bad cells through, smoothing can't move them out, solver diverges anyway. **Default-patch trap (v29 lesson)**: removing a body from sHM `geometry { regions { ... } }` and from `refinementSurfaces` does NOT exclude its faces from the mesh — sHM falls back to a synthetic patch name `<geomName>_<bodyName>` and meshes the body at default refinement. To truly suppress a body, edit the STL itself. **Local heuristic gap**: `07_check_mesh.py` advice ("→ nSolveIter ↑") is correct for diffuse skew but misses both the patch-concentration signature AND the default-patch trap. Future versions should (a) detect single-patch concentration ≥80% → suggest STL inspection; (b) detect `<geomName>_` prefix in skew face patches → suggest STL body removal. **Inheritance**: any industrial case with multiple STL bodies + hand-edited sHM dict + dropping a patch inherits the default-patch trap. **Counter row**: autonomous_governance: true (F1+F2+F2.1 all landed in-session as config + STL surgery; final residue 56 skew faces on Outer_Surf is the v24-class diffuse problem, not addressable by this same playbook) |

### V76 · Squeezing the balloon — refinement bumps move skewness prominence between patches without eliminating it

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | 2026-05-12 follow-up to V75: after F2.1 STL surgery left 56 diffuse skew faces with Outer_Surf holding 57% (32 faces), F3a bumped Outer_Surf refinement (1,2) → (2,3) (40mm → 20mm cells). v31 rebuild result: cell count **2.42M → 3.10M (+28%)**, skew face count **56 → 30 (−46%)**, **but max_skewness went UP from 7.624 → 7.829**. Outer_Surf disappeared from top patches; prominence moved to **firewall_behind (23%, 7 faces) + body_4 (23%, 7 faces)** — patches that held only ~12% of v30's residue |
| Engineer symptom | F3a "worked" on its target — Outer_Surf is no longer the hotspot. But the verdict-gate metric (max_skewness) didn't improve; it slightly degraded. The remaining 30 skew faces are distributed across 8+ patches; no single fix can address them. Engineer following the V75 playbook (locate dominant patch → fix) finds themselves in an infinite loop of patch-by-patch refinement bumps, each producing minor count reduction but no decisive PASS |
| Root cause | **Diffuse-residue mesh quality has no single intervention class.** Once V75-class fixes have eliminated the dominant-hotspot patches (APU_door 95% → resolved), the remaining skew is distributed by the underlying STL geometry: each input body has its own snap-stress profile based on triangle aspect ratio, curvature continuity, and intersection with neighboring bodies. Bumping refinement on patch X reduces snap stress at X but exposes the next worst patch as the new hotspot. The fundamental limit is the input STL triangulation quality — Outer_Surf has 576 triangles with median aspect 20.84 (strip-like panels typical of CAD airframe exports). No amount of sHM tuning can produce isotropic cells from anisotropic input triangles |
| Fix | **F3a (landed 2026-05-12)**: `case/system/snappyHexMeshDict` line 109 `Outer_Surf level (1 2) → (2 3)`; `config/case.yaml` line 75 mirror update. Result: 46% skew count reduction with 28% cell count growth; max_skewness slightly worse. **F3b/c (chosen NOT to pursue)**: same fix on firewall_behind + body_4 would likely produce same shape — count down, prominence elsewhere, max_skewness oscillates. **F4 (deferred to dedicated session)**: either (a) full STL remesh with isotropic triangulation (PyMeshLab `meshing_repair` + `meshing_remove_duplicate_faces` + `meshing_isotropic_explicit_remeshing` on each body), or (b) relax `meshQualityControls.maxSkewness 4 → 6` in sHM (accept current mesh quality, run solver, see if convergence is achievable) |
| Status | F3a landed (partial); F4 deferred. Mesh state: v31 = 3.10M cells, max_skewness 7.829, 30 skew faces (best topology since v24). Adequate for relaxed-controls solver run; below baseline for strict-controls validation |
| Reference case | APU bay case_002a · v30 → v31 mesh iteration · 2026-05-12 |
| Lesson | **"Squeezing the balloon"**: after the dominant-hotspot patch is fixed (V75 class), per-patch refinement bumps cannot break through to mesh PASS because each fix just moves the next-worst patch into prominence. The lever shape is fundamentally different from V75: V75 fix had a concentrated source (one body); V76 residue has a distributed source (input STL geometry across N bodies). **Diagnostic pattern**: if the dominant patch holds < 30% of skew faces, refinement bumps are unlikely to deliver PASS — that's the V76 signal to switch intervention class. **Decision tree** (post-V75 fix): dominant patch ≥ 80% → V75 playbook (STL surgery on that body); dominant patch 30-80% → mixed; try refinement bump, expect partial fix, reassess; dominant patch < 30% → V76 territory; refinement bumps won't help — either remesh all STLs OR relax meshQualityControls. **STL aspect-ratio ceiling**: for industrial CAD-exported airframe / shell geometry with median triangle aspect > 10, sHM cannot produce mesh below max_skewness ≈ 5-8 without remeshing the input. This is independent of refinement levels. **Sub-pattern of Pattern 3 (mesh quality numerical safety margin)**: cross-references existing V8/Pattern-3 finding that loose meshQualityControls let bad cells contaminate downstream — but adds the converse: TIGHT controls on geometrically-inadequate STL just produce mesh build failures or perpetual FAIL verdicts. **Counter row**: autonomous_governance: true (F3a landed as 2-line config; F4 requires user judgment on whether to remesh STLs or relax controls — sub-DEC class if implemented) |

### V77 · Isotropic STL remesh — works on simple shells, destroys complex feature bodies

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | 2026-05-12 follow-up to V75/V76: per-body STL aspect-ratio scan on APU bay v31 combined STL identified 12 bodies needing remesh (4 BAD median>30, 8 ELONGATED median 10-30) plus 12 GOOD/OK-ish that should stay as-is. v31 hotspots (firewall_behind 23%, body_4 23%) were precisely on ELONGATED bodies, confirming V76's STL-aspect-ratio thesis. F4a attempt: PyMeshLab `meshing_isotropic_explicit_remeshing` with `targetlen=PureValue(0.10)`, `collapseflag=True`, `adaptive=False` applied uniformly to all 12. **Outcome split sharply by body class** |
| Engineer symptom | The 6 simple shell bodies remeshed dramatically: Inner_Surf 576→4656 tri (8× growth) median aspect 26→2.29; Outer_Surf 576→4610 (8×) 20.84→1.94; firewall_front 288→1006 (3.5×) 11.16→1.42; firewall_behind 288→2384 (8×) 10.30→1.38; apu_intake 34→508 (15×) 185.51→1.40; farfield_cylinder 384→8278 (22×) 36.69→1.35. **All became near-equilateral**. But the 6 complex feature bodies were destroyed: body_4 64576→1 tri (collapsed to single triangle!); body_6 82142→2112 (lost 97%); body_8 217898→2380 (lost 99%); p_2 23810→1568 (lost 93%); combustor_outlet 1954→346 (lost 82%); body_5 716→372 (lost 48%). The aggressive `collapseflag=True` + 50mm target on small (<1m bbox) high-detail bodies (body_8 spans 0.30×0.57×0.57m with 217k tri = avg 1.2mm features) collapses all sub-target edges → entire body dissolves |
| Root cause | **Isotropic remesh assumes the input is "over-tessellated with bad triangle shapes" — true for CAD shells exported as long strips, false for CAD bodies with rich feature detail.** Simple shells (firewall plate, intake louver, domain cylinder) have NO sub-millimeter features; strip triangles are decorative — replacing with isotropic 100mm tiles loses nothing. Complex bodies (collector cavity walls with bolt holes, manifold curves with fillets) have features AT the strip scale; the "long edges" of CAD strips actually trace real curvature transitions. Collapsing them destroys the geometry. **Default `collapseflag=True` is the trap**: it cannot distinguish "long edge that's a stylistic CAD artifact" from "long edge that traces a feature curvature transition" |
| Fix | **F4a-strict (landed 2026-05-12)**: only remesh 6 safe simple shells (Inner_Surf, Outer_Surf, firewall_{front,behind}, apu_intake, farfield_cylinder); skip 6 complex bodies; concatenate remeshed + verbatim bodies back into combined STL. Combined STL grew 290 → 294 MB (+1.4%). v32 mesh rebuild result: cell count flat (3.10M), **max_skewness 7.829 → 6.875** (FIRST time downward trend in 5 iterations), skew face count **30 → 20**, under-determined cells **95 → 46 (−51%)**. Shells effectively gone from top patches (Outer_Surf 32→1 across v30→v32; firewall_behind 7→0); residue 8 faces on body_4 + 2 body_8 + scattered on bodies we COULDN'T remesh. **F4a-adaptive (smoke-tested, not landed)**: PyMeshLab `adaptive=True` + `collapseflag=False` + 3mm target on body_8 produced 217k→752k tri (3.45× growth) with median aspect 26→4.39, but max aspect 574 (some bad outliers remain). Useful but expensive — combined STL would grow ~500MB. **F4b (deferred · 1-line)**: relax `meshQualityControls.maxSkewness 4 → 8` in sHM dict to accept v32 mesh; allows solver to attempt convergence on current geometry |
| Status | F4a-strict landed; F4a-adaptive smoke-tested but not deployed; F4b deferred. **Mesh state v32 = 3.10M cells · max_skewness 6.875 · 20 skew faces · 3 failed checks** — adequate for relaxed-controls solver attempt; below baseline for strict-controls verification. Total reduction from v27 baseline: skew faces 1119→20 (98.2%), max_skewness 7.999→6.875 (delivered the first 1.1 of the 3.6 needed to reach PASS threshold) |
| Reference case | APU bay case_002a · v31 → v32 mesh iteration · 2026-05-12 |
| Lesson | **Triangle-shape pathology is bimodal**: stylistic (long strips with no underlying feature curvature) vs structural (long edges tracing real curvature transitions). Isotropic remesh with edge collapse can clean stylistic but destroys structural — and there's no automatic way to tell which is which from triangle stats alone (median aspect 11 could be either). **Per-body manual classification needed**: simple shells (median aspect > 10 + few thousand tri + simple shape) → remesh; complex bodies (≥10k tri + sub-bbox feature detail) → preserve. **PyMeshLab parameter tuning**: `collapseflag=False` + `adaptive=True` + matched-to-feature target edge can sometimes salvage complex bodies (cf. body_8 smoke test), but rapidly inflates tri count. **Diagnostic pattern**: before remeshing, compute per-body bbox span / sqrt(tri_count) = average feature scale; if target_edge_length > 2× feature_scale → don't remesh. **STL surgery ceiling**: even after V75 (drop overlapping body) + V77 (remesh simple shells), industrial CAD-exported geometry will have ~5-10% irreducible skew face residue on complex bodies. To bring max_skewness < 4 PASS without remeshing complex bodies, either (a) use a different mesher (cfMesh / gmsh / fluent) with different snap algorithm, or (b) relax sHM `meshQualityControls`. For most production CFD workflows, (b) is the pragmatic path — the solver itself is robust to skewness 5-7 if numerical schemes are chosen carefully (limited grad, deferred correction). **Cross-reference Pattern 3**: V77 makes the "loose mesh quality controls" trade-off explicit — sometimes the right answer is loosen the gate, not push the mesh harder. **Counter row**: autonomous_governance: true (F4a-strict landed as STL surgery; F4a-adaptive + F4b deferred — sub-DEC class if pursued) |

### V78 · sHM handles CAD overlap better than jagged hole boundaries — bbox shell-punch surgery backfires (negative finding)

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | 2026-05-12 follow-up to V77: pairwise interleave analysis on APU bay v32 STL (16 non-APU bodies, all topologically clean) found **33 pairs with real triangle-triangle intersections** — classic industrial CAD→STL pattern. Worst: Inner_Surf×p_1 (1542 faces, 33% of Inner_Surf), Inner_Surf×firewall_behind (476), Outer_Surf×firewall_behind (304), Inner_Surf×Frame_1 (301, 52% of Frame_1 buried). Hypothesis (F5): punch clean holes in Inner_Surf+Outer_Surf where piercing bodies sit. Approach: delete shell triangles whose centroid is inside piercing body's bbox (shrunk 10mm). Result: Inner_Surf 4656→3970 (15% removed), Outer_Surf 4610→4448 (3.5%). Re-verification: intersection counts dropped 13-67% across worst pairs (Inner_Surf×p_1: 1542→507). **Should have improved mesh quality** — instead v33 rebuild REGRESSED: max_skewness 6.875→7.966 (worse, near 8.0 wall again), skew face count 20→69 (+245%), under-determined cells 46→118 (+156%), total cells 3.10M→3.97M (+28%). New skew prominence: Outer_Surf 37.7% + Inner_Surf 29% = **67% of skew now on the bodies we surgically modified** |
| Engineer symptom | Plausible-looking pre-surgery diagnostic (intersection face counts dropped, geometric overlap cleaned) led to worse mesh quality. Naive reading of V75/V76/V77 might suggest "STL overlap is bad → punch holes → mesh improves". Empirical lesson is the OPPOSITE: **sHM handles topologically-valid CAD overlap better than topologically-invalid jagged hole boundaries** |
| Root cause | **sHM's snap algorithm is designed for CONTINUOUS surfaces.** CAD-exported bodies that interpenetrate produce smooth implicit intersection curves; sHM's snap projects each cell vertex toward the nearest surface, and the implicit intersection is geometrically clean. **Jagged hole boundaries** (bbox-based triangle deletion result) produce non-smooth discontinuities — each deleted triangle leaves dangling edges sHM cannot snap to cleanly. The 686 Inner_Surf triangles deleted leave 686 dangling edges; the algorithm cannot interpolate across these gaps, producing 20 new skew faces on Inner_Surf and 26 on Outer_Surf. **Surgery introduced more problems than the overlap it removed** |
| Fix | **F5 (landed and reverted 2026-05-12)**: STL surgery applied, regression measured, STL+polyMesh reverted to v32 state. v32 (3.10M cells, max_skewness 6.875, 20 skew faces) remains the actual best state. **F5-proper (NOT pursued)**: Boolean-difference on closed shells with smooth re-meshing of resulting boundaries. Multi-hour multi-step; uncertain payoff because complex body_4 hotspot wouldn't be touched anyway |
| Status | F5 attempted, regression measured, reverted. **v32 stands as best APU bay case_002a mesh state** (3.10M cells, max_skewness 6.875, 20 skew faces). Final intervention class proven empirically — STL surgery has hard boundaries beyond which it makes things worse |
| Reference case | APU bay case_002a · v32 → v33 attempted regression · 2026-05-12 |
| Lesson | **Counter-intuitive but empirically validated: STL inter-body overlap (continuous, implicit intersection curve) is BETTER for sHM than STL surgery with bbox-deleted approximate holes (discontinuous, jagged boundary).** This INVERTS the naive intuition "overlap is bad, clean it". The 33-pair intersection map in v32 was a RED HERRING — it described real CAD pathology but it was NOT the dominant source of mesh-quality issues. The 20 v32 skew faces were on COMPLEX bodies (body_4 40%) with internal feature complexity, not on the interpenetration zones. **Diagnostic pattern**: before doing STL surgery to "clean" apparent overlaps, FIRST verify whether those overlaps contribute to skew faces (per-patch skew localization on the unmodified mesh). If overlap regions are NOT in the skew hotspot list, leave them alone — sHM handles them. Surgery to "clean" non-problematic overlaps creates new boundaries that ARE problems. **Surgical safety rule**: STL edits must produce SMOOTH boundaries (Boolean operations on closed meshes, or remesh after edit), never approximate bbox deletion on open shells. **Combined V77+V78 message**: for industrial CFD STL prep, the safe interventions are (a) remesh elongated simple shells with isotropic equilateral triangles (V77), and (b) drop entire STL bodies you don't need (V75 F2.1). Everything else risks regression. **Counter row**: autonomous_governance: true (F5 attempted + reverted in-session; no DEC needed for negative-finding sediment) |

### V79 · First D7 injection — `face_orientation` advisor gap surfaced (no detector landed) [backfill 2026-05-12 for case_012 v1 2026-05-09 · closed 2026-05-13 by DEC-V61-198-sub-A4]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | case_012 D7: `louver_vane_2` rotated 38.000° from its intended orientation inside the 4-way ceiling diffuser louver bank. `defect_manifest.yaml` `expected_advisor_to_catch: none_landed`; manual FreeCAD verification by `scripts/check_face_normal.py` confirmed the vane's face normal differs from sibling vanes' by exactly 38° (env-var-driven exact match) |
| Engineer symptom | No advisor in the harness detects "a face/body that should have a specific orientation has been rotated". A2 detects shared interfaces; thin_wall_advisor detects extreme-thinness; geometry_surgery decimates faces; A4 face-normal-dot-against-sibling is **not** implemented. sHM happily meshes the rotated vane; downstream the engineer sees diffuser-jet asymmetry only after running the full solver and comparing throw-distance T_50 to the symmetric-vanes baseline. Component_bank lists D7 with engineering symptom "sHM `locationInMesh` ambiguity" — case_012 confirms the milder symptom (silent flow-pattern asymmetry, not mesh failure) |
| Root cause | The advisor stack assumes face-orientation is preserved by CAD export. A "rotated component" is a different defect class — closer to assembly-instruction violation than CAD-quality inspection. No advisor module covers this class; the closest existing primitive is `find_face_facing_target` (used by A2 `_run_shared`) but that consumes orientation as input, not as a defect signal |
| Fix | (1) **Case-local**: manual `check_face_normal.py` verification documents D7 explicitly with env-var args (no main-session-driven path); sub-session does NOT skip verification (Hard Guardrail #3 enforcement). (2) **Advisor candidate (A4 face-orientation)**: given parts manifest with `expected_face_normal` field (or sibling-group declaration), flag any body whose dominant face normal deviates from declared/sibling-consensus by more than a per-group tolerance (default 5°). Two-case validation required before promoting to `confirmed`. (3) **harvest 003 candidate** — flag for evaluation in next harvest cycle alongside V55 (D6 / A5) + V56 (D9 / A6) |
| Status | **[VALIDATED] 2026-05-13** — A4 `face_orientation_advisor` landed (DEC-V61-198-sub-A4) closes the claim "no advisor catches D7 face-orientation defects". V79 + V87 cross-topology evidence pair (case_012 whole-vane Z-axis 38° + case_013 LE-chunk XY-axis 22°) both reproducible via the productized `check_face_orientation` API: when caller supplies `actual_face_normal` (extracted upstream via FreeCAD `face.normalAt()` for Z-axis topology or tilt-from-XY-plane signature for chord-axis topology), the advisor flags both at `severity=critical`. Regression tests at `ui/backend/tests/test_face_orientation_advisor.py::test_case_012_louver_vane_2_regression` + `::test_case_013_blade_3_regression` pin the ground-truth measurements (38.000° + 21.979°) |
| Reference case | case_012_hvac_supply_diffuser (`scripts/check_face_normal.py` 2026-05-09; `evidence/v1/defect_verification.json` D7 block; case profile §"Defects" line 73 — measured 38.000°) |
| Lesson | First **component-orientation** defect class surfaces an advisor-stack scope gap parallel to V55 (extra-body) and V56 (curved-tessellation). Pattern: case_012 is the **third advisor-stack-scope-audit case** by accident — the brief did not flag it as one, but it surfaced a first-injection D7 that no advisor catches. **Backfill note**: this row was missing when case_012 sub-session sedimented 2026-05-09 (V49-V53 were case_015/case_016 findings, not case_012). Inserted 2026-05-12 from main session for V-series consistency — D6/D7/D9/D10 first-injection rows now parallel-formatted as V55/V79/V56/(D10 pending case_020). **Numbering note**: originally allocated as V78 in commit 2b77e22 but collided with V78 (sHM-overlap negative finding, commit e25d0de same day from APU bay session); renumbered to V79 within minutes. **2026-05-13 closure**: A4 lands as a pure dict-consumer (mirrors A5 inlet_outlet_validator pattern) — FreeCAD normal extraction stays upstream/caller-side, keeping the advisor side-effect-free and test-isolated. The productized form accepts both Z-axis (case_012) and XY-axis (case_013) rotation topologies via the topology-agnostic `abs(dot(actual, intended))` measurement, defaulting to per-axis median for sibling-consensus mode (robust to single outlier per draft §7 R1). **Counter row**: autonomous_governance: true (cross-topology evidence pair met without further dispatch; sub-DEC scope additive new module + 9-test suite + V79/V87 status flip — no schema break, no auth/signing/security boundary, v2.3 §2 1-sync-trigger does not fire) |

### V80 · STEP `FILE_NAME` wall-clock timestamp breaks byte-determinism of OCP-exported CAD [backfill 2026-05-13 for case_012 v1 2026-05-09]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | cadquery 2.7.0 + OCP STEP exporter writes the build wall-clock timestamp into the `FILE_NAME(...)` header line. Two byte-identical-geometry runs of the same `build_cad.py` produce different SHA-256 hashes. Observed in 4 case sub-sessions: case_002a (APU bay v* iterations), case_005 (RAE M2129), case_011 (compact HX), case_012 (HVAC diffuser). case_012's `scripts/build_cad.py::canonicalize_step` is the first case-local workaround |
| Engineer symptom | The Codex case-design protocol's determinism contract requires that the same CAD generator produce byte-identical STEP output across runs (for caching, golden-snapshot regression, and downstream advisor input invariance). Without canonicalization, every regeneration changes the SHA-256, defeating any byte-deterministic audit |
| Root cause | OCP's STEP exporter populates the ISO-10303-21 `FILE_NAME` header's 2nd argument (`time_stamp`, per the standard) with the current wall clock. The geometry data (CARTESIAN_POINT, EDGE_CURVE, etc.) is fully deterministic; only the header timestamp is volatile. This is a STEP-spec-correct behavior — the spec mandates `time_stamp` but does NOT require it be wall-clock — so a sentinel value is fully compliant |
| Fix | (1) **Case-local workaround**: case_012 `scripts/build_cad.py::canonicalize_step` rewrites the FILE_NAME line replacing the timestamp with `'1970-01-01T00:00:00'`. Aggressive — truncates the rest of FILE_NAME at the author comma. (2) **A7 productized form (LANDED 2026-05-12 by DEC-V61-198-sub-A7)**: `ui/backend/services/geometry_ingest/step_canonicalizer.py` with `canonicalize_step_text` / `canonicalize_step_file` / `StepCanonicalizationReport`. Surgical regex replacement on the 2nd quoted arg of `FILE_NAME(...)`, preserving author / organization / preprocessor_version / originating_system / authorisation fields. Idempotent via `is_already_canonical` signal. Conservative first-pass: only `FILE_NAME` timestamp; `FILE_DESCRIPTION` left untouched per draft patch open-question resolution. (3) **Codex case-design protocol amendment**: future case briefs should call `step_canonicalizer.canonicalize_step_file(path, inplace=True)` from `build_cad.py::main()` immediately after `asm.save(...exportType='STEP')`. Reference impl in case_012; pattern replicable across all cadquery cases |
| Status | **closed 2026-05-12 by DEC-V61-198-sub-A7** — A7 lands as harvest-003 priority #3. 10-test suite (canonicalize_text replacement / idempotency / no-FILE_NAME / custom-sentinel / preserves-other-fields / preserves-geometry / file-inplace-byte-determinism / file-not-inplace-sibling / already-canonical-signal / report-dataclass-shape). All 10 green. Cross-case retroactive application: future case sub-sessions can apply `canonicalize_step_file` to existing sandbox STEP files to retrofit byte-determinism without regenerating |
| Reference case | case_012_hvac_supply_diffuser v1 sediment 2026-05-09 (`scripts/build_cad.py:233-247`); cross-cuts observed in case_002a / case_005 / case_011 (all OCP-exported CadQuery). **Backfill note**: this row was missing when case_012 sedimented — case_index claimed "V49-V53" but those V-numbers were assigned to case_015/case_016 work that landed 2026-05-10/11. Inserted 2026-05-13 from main session at A7 landing time |
| Lesson | Determinism failures from third-party libraries (OCP timestamp) are best fixed at the **last stage before bytes-out**, not at the source of generation. The fix is content-preserving (geometry untouched) and STEP-spec-compliant (sentinel is a valid time_stamp value). **Pillar-of-good-fix shape**: surgical regex on a single header field, dataclass-based report for caller inspection, opt-in `inplace` flag for safe non-destructive operation. **Counter row**: autonomous_governance: true (A7 landed as productized advisor; no Codex re-iteration needed under round-cap=3 since this is sub-DEC scope with full test coverage and no auth/signing/security boundary) |

### V81 · Codex CAD inlet/outlet emitted as 3D solid bodies → sHM treats as walls → no patch surface created → v1 ran as silent sealed-room natural convection [backfill 2026-05-13 for case_012 v1 2026-05-09]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | case_012 v1 sediment 2026-05-09. Codex's engineering brief specified `supply_inlet` (T=289.15K, U=2.6 m/s, slot 10mm) + `return_outlet` (pressureOutlet, low-side-wall). CAD generator emitted both as `cq.Workplane().box(L, W, H)` → 3D solid bodies in STEP. STL extraction produced closed surfaces. sHM treated the closed surfaces as walls. Resulting mesh has NO inlet/outlet patch — `boundary` dict shows only ceiling/floor/walls. v1 ran as natural-convection-only sealed room with 500W internal heat sources; ADPI / throw / dumping metrics in v1 REPORT.md cannot be compared to the brief's ASHRAE 55 design table |
| Engineer symptom | Codex case-design protocol's validation checklist (`codex_case_design_protocol.md` pre-amendment) had 6 checks (CadQuery executes / STEP imports / names match / defects exist / patch-name regex / solver-class match) — none of them caught the inlet/outlet emission pattern mismatch. The first signal was at v1 sediment when REPORT.md author noticed ASHRAE 55 metrics could not be the headline because there was no jet to throw |
| Root cause | The protocol's "Approved CAD emission patterns" did not constrain boundary geometry — it implicitly assumed all parts_manifest entries become 3D solids (the default cq.Assembly pattern). For wall-bounded volumes (rooms, cavities, ducts) this is correct; for fluid through-flow boundaries it collapses the patch entirely because sHM's snap algorithm cannot tell "this thin box is meant to be an opening" from "this thin box is meant to be a wall" — both are closed surfaces to it |
| Fix | (1) **Protocol amendment (LANDED 2026-05-12 by DEC-V61-198-sub-protocol-inlet-outlet)**: new §"Inlet/outlet boundary geometry emission" with three approved patterns — (a) thin-extrusion 1 mm + `boundary_emission: thin_extrusion` annotation in parts_manifest, (b) `createPatch` carve via `boundary_zones` metadata, (c) reserved faceZones experimental. New validation step 7 (boundary-zone audit) in `codex_case_design_protocol.md::Main session validation step`. (2) **Sealed-room honest annotation**: if the brief genuinely is sealed-room natural-convection, parts_manifest carries `boundary_emission: sealed_room_natural_convection` to make the relaxation explicit (case_012 v1 retroactively annotated; v1.5 must address). (3) **A5-class validator (LANDED 2026-05-13 by DEC-V61-198-sub-A5-inlet-outlet-validator)**: `ui/backend/services/geometry_ingest/inlet_outlet_validator.py` + 9-test suite. API `validate_inlet_outlet_emission(parts_manifest) -> InletOutletValidationReport` audits each through-flow body (role ∈ {supply, return, inlet, outlet}) against the three approved patterns and emits per-body severity findings. Catches the V81 failure mode pre-mesh instead of at v1 sediment. (4) **Retroactive audit guidance**: every dispatched-but-not-sediment case with through-flow boundaries (case_013/015/017/018/019/020) inherits the risk; main session manually checks each CadQuery script for Pattern 1/2/3 compliance before sub-session dispatch (A5 validator can now run on the parts_manifest before dispatch when one is provided) |
| Status | **closed 2026-05-13 · A5-validator landed (DEC-V61-198-sub-A5-inlet-outlet-validator)** — protocol amended 2026-05-12; auto-validator landed 2026-05-13; **retroactive case audit deferred** to per-case sub-session-time inspection (A5 validator is the automated tool main session now runs at dispatch when parts_manifest is available). case_012 v1.5 fix path documented (createPatch carve) in case profile but not yet executed; case_012 v1 evidence remains honest-evidence sealed-room |
| Reference case | case_012_hvac_supply_diffuser v1 sediment 2026-05-09; draft patch `.planning/patches/draft_codex_cad_inlet_outlet_protocol_amendment_2026-05-09.md` (harvest cycle 003 cycle 002 origin) |
| Lesson | Validation checklists must cover both **what's there** (CadQuery executes, STEP imports, names match) AND **what's emitted shape** (3D solid vs thin-extrusion vs metadata-only). The pre-amendment 6-check list answered "did Codex deliver?" but not "did Codex deliver the right *kind* of geometry for each role?" This is a meta-lesson for protocol design: when a generator's emission shape encodes role-specific physics semantics (wall vs opening), the validation must inspect emission shape per role, not just emission existence. **Pattern 4 candidate (silent-fail-class)**: similar to V47 minMedialAxisAngle typo, V48 helper-solid-offset — the pipeline accepts wrong-but-syntactically-valid input. **Counter row**: autonomous_governance: true (protocol amendment landed; validator script deferred — that's the autonomous boundary, automation comes later when ≥ 2 cases hit the same failure mode and overdetermine the script's design) |

### V82 · STL coordinate-scale mismatch (STL in mm, blockMesh in m) → sHM surface intersection = 0 → mesh contains zero vehicle [backfill 2026-05-13 for case_010 v1 2026-05-08 · supersedes V46 primary attribution]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | case_010 v1 sediment 2026-05-08, surfaced retroactively by Track C advisor session 2026-05-13. `01_extract_stl.py` calls `cq.exporters.export(cq.Workplane().add(shape), …, exportType="STL")` on shapes whose native unit is **mm** (per `build_cad.py` L_REF_MM=4610). CadQuery STL export preserves the shape's native unit verbatim. `system/snappyHexMeshDict` `geometry { drivaer.stl { type triSurfaceMesh; … } }` block has **no** `scale (0.001 0.001 0.001)` transform. blockMesh / blockMeshDict uses scale=1 with vertex coords in **m**. Therefore sHM treats STL coordinates as m, and the vehicle-bbox max (4610) lives at 4610 m — ~76× past the domain `xMax=41.49` m. eMesh `boundingBox: (0 −1.5e−14 0.10) (4610 1120.85 1444.28)` in sHM log line 39 is the load-bearing diagnostic |
| Engineer symptom | `case/log/03_snappyHexMesh.log` shows: "Read mesh in 10.37 s · Overall mesh bounding box (−18.44 0 0) (41.49 13.83 23.05)" (domain in m) then "Read edgeMesh drivaer.eMesh … boundingBox (0 −1.5e−14 0.10) (4610 1120.85 1444.28)" (vehicle in 4610-something). Surface refinement iter 0: "Marked for refinement due to surface intersection: 0 cells. … Selected for refinement: 0 cells (out of 4644000). Stopping refining since too few cells selected." 0 intersected edges across all subsequent edge-intersection tests. sHM still added the 6 wall patches (vehicle_body / wheels / mirror / trim / underbody) but they have **zero faces** post-castellation. `evidence/v1/check_mesh_summary.json` reports `mesh_ok=True, n_cells=4,644,000 (= blockMesh exactly), max_skewness=3e−13, max_aspect_ratio=1.006, min_volume == max_volume to 9 digits` — the dishonest-clean signature of a uniform hex grid with no geometry inside |
| Root cause | Two-part: **(a)** CadQuery `cq.exporters.export(..., exportType="STL")` is unit-preserving — it inherits the workplane's native unit. `build_cad.py` works in mm without applying a `* 0.001` scale before export. **(b)** `system/snappyHexMeshDict` `geometry.drivaer.stl` block omits `scale (0.001 0.001 0.001);` — the canonical OpenFOAM idiom for "STL was authored in mm, read in m". Either fix alone closes the bug. The pattern is structurally identical to V20 (Tier-1 STEP unit-context loss on cadquery roundtrip) but at a different pipeline stage: V20 is upstream (STEP → cq import); V82 is downstream (cq → STL → sHM). V46 attributed v1 mesh outcome to "base cell too fine + container wall-clock" — wrong: even with completed sHM at base cell 0.30 m, the mesh would still contain zero vehicle because the surface refinement input is wrong. The "cell explosion" V46 observed at Shell refinement iter 0-2 (4.6M → 4.86M → 6.55M) is the `refine_wake_box` (level 3 inside (4.61 0 0)−(23.05 4.61 1.5)) cascading through `nCellsBetweenLevels=3` buffer — independent of any STL intersection |
| Fix | Any one of: **(1) `scale (0.001 0.001 0.001);` inside `geometry { drivaer.stl { … } }` in snappyHexMeshDict** (and same for `drivaer.eMesh` consumer — `surfaceFeatureExtractDict` writes eMesh in input STL's unit). **(2) Rewrite STL in m**: scale the CadQuery shapes by 0.001 in `01_extract_stl.py` before `cq.exporters.export(...)`. **(3) OpenFOAM offline tool**: `surfaceTransformPoints -scale '(0.001 0.001 0.001)' drivaer.stl drivaer_m.stl` + same for the eMesh path. Per-case sub-session action; main-project lesson is the cross-case pattern below |
| Status | **fix-verified 2026-05-13 · 1 case** — case_010 v1.5 re-mesh confirms V82 hypothesis. Applied fix path (3) `surfaceTransformPoints -scale '(0.001 0.001 0.001)' drivaer.stl drivaer_m.stl` + re-ran `surfaceFeatureExtract` → new eMesh `boundingBox: (0, −1.5e−17, 0.00010257) (4.61, 1.12085, 1.44428)` (m, was 4610 in mm). sHM re-run: surface refinement iter 0 marked 20,749 cells (was 0), iter 1-4 marked 32,279 → 31,315 → 20,161 → 3 cells; intersected edges grew 911 → 2,408 → 6,410 → 14,408 → 33,357 → 67,641 → 120k → 187k → 247k → 291k → 345k (was 0 throughout baseline); cells per refinement level shows levels 0/1/2/3/4/5/6 active (baseline had only level 0). Mesh grew 4.64M → 21.4M cells, hit `maxGlobalCells=20,000,000` cap mid Shell refinement iter 3. **Primary V82 obstruction closed; V46's secondary "base cell too fine / cell budget" obstruction is now the active one** — exactly as the V82 lesson predicted (V46 retained its legitimate scaling lesson, just attached to the wrong primary cause). Evidence preserved in `~/Desktop/case_010_drivaer_fastback_les/case/log/v1_baseline/` (baseline logs) vs `case/log/03_snappyHexMesh.log` (fixed run) + `constant/triSurface/drivaer.stl.bak_mm` (baseline STL). **case_007 probe 2026-05-13 (negative)**: case_007_kcs_ship_vof sHM log eMesh `boundingBox: (−3.64, 0, −0.34) (4.13, 0.57, 0.26)` — m-scale (KCS scale-model LPP=7.28 m). Intersected edges progress 0 → 439 → 1,481 → ... → 13,044; surface refinement marked cells 1,450 → 547 → 6 → 0. **case_007 does NOT reproduce V82**. **Differential**: case_007 has 2-stage pipeline `build_cad → STEP (mm-native) → 04_step_to_stl.py (explicit *0.001 conversion to m)` — `04_step_to_stl.py` line 4 comment says "Output: inputs/kcs_hull_combined.stl (units in meters; STEP is in mm)" and line 63 `Cache nodes (apply transform; convert mm -> m by *0.001)`. case_010 has 1-stage pipeline `build_cad → 01_extract_stl.py (cq.exporters.export STL direct, no scale conversion)`. **V82 failure mode is pipeline-shape-specific**: cq-direct-export path → bug; STEP→STL-bridge path → safe. **Pillar-2 trigger does NOT fire** (1 case observed + 1 differential safe-case, not 2 same-class cases). `surface_scale_advisor` extraction deferred until either (a) a 2nd cq-direct-export case reproduces, or (b) a STEP→STL-bridge case nevertheless fails (would indicate a deeper pattern). case_004 / case_008 sandboxes have no completed sHM run (only advisor JSON evidence) so cannot field-validate either way; case_005/006/009/011/012/015/016 each need a per-case sHM-log probe (single grep) before dispatch of any new sub-session using their pipeline pattern |
| Reference case | case_010_drivaer_fastback_les v1 baseline 2026-05-08 (`~/Desktop/case_010_drivaer_fastback_les/case/log/03_snappyHexMesh.log` line 39 eMesh bbox; `case/log/03_snappyHexMesh.log` "Marked for refinement due to surface intersection: 0 cells"; `case/system/snappyHexMeshDict` geometry block sans scale; `case/constant/triSurface/drivaer.stl` head vertex `(2.94e+02, 6.55e+02, 1.69e+02)` in mm). Track C retro: `.planning/retrospectives/2026-05-13_track_c_advisor_e2e_session_1_case_010.md` |
| Lesson | When a multi-stage CAD ingest crosses a unit-convention boundary (CadQuery-native mm → OpenFOAM-native m), the **bridge step needs explicit scale documentation**. CadQuery's `cq.exporters.export(..., exportType="STL")` is unit-preserving (correct behavior); OpenFOAM's `triSurfaceMesh` is unit-agnostic (treats coords as the same unit as blockMesh). The mismatch is silent — `blockMesh OK`, `surfaceFeatureExtract OK`, `snappyHexMesh OK` (with zero intersections, but still exits 0), `checkMesh OK` (the blockMesh is geometrically valid). The only load-bearing diagnostic is the **eMesh bounding box vs domain bounding box ratio** in the sHM log. **Methodology consequence**: case authors and corpus sedimentation pipelines (V46 missed this) need a "mesh contains intended geometry" check — see V83. **Pattern 4 (silent-fail) sibling**: V47 minMedialAxisAngle typo, V48 helper-solid-offset, V81 inlet/outlet-as-solid — all four cases where the pipeline accepts wrong-but-syntactically-valid input and produces a mesh that looks numerically clean but is physically wrong. **Track C session 1 finding**: this V-row is the first finding produced by Claude Code session acting as advisor over a sedimented case — it surfaces a load-bearing root cause that the sub-session author's audit + V46 sedimentation pipeline missed. Validates the M6 advisor concept at upper-bound (full corpus + log Read access); does NOT validate a constrained RAG-retrieval N6.2 route surface. **Pipeline-shape pattern lesson (2026-05-13 from case_007 differential)**: the failure mode is **bound to the cq-direct-export pipeline shape** (`cq.exporters.export(shape, exportType="STL")` with no explicit scale), NOT to CadQuery or OpenFOAM individually. STEP-bridge pipelines (`build_cad → STEP → 04_step_to_stl.py with *0.001`) are safe because the bridge author necessarily confronts the mm→m conversion when reading the STEP file's `LENGTH_UNIT_MILLIMETRE` header. case_007 is the proof-of-safe path; case_010 is the proof-of-unsafe path. **Codex case-design protocol amendment candidate**: every new CadQuery-based `build_cad.py` MUST be paired with an extraction script that either (a) routes through STEP→STL bridge OR (b) applies explicit `cq.exporters.export(scaled_shape, ...)` where scaled_shape has been `cq.Workplane().add(shape).newObject([shape.scale(0.001)])`-ed. This is a single-line check at code-review time, not yet a Codex case-design protocol clause — deferred until 2nd case triggers Pillar-2 or a Codex pre-dispatch lint step is justified |

### V83 · `mesh_ok=true` in `check_mesh_summary.json` does not cover "geometry-derived wall patches have zero faces" → silent acceptance of meshes with no intended geometry [backfill 2026-05-13 for case_010 v1 2026-05-08 · methodology gap]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | `~/Desktop/case_010_drivaer_fastback_les/scripts/07_check_mesh.py` and `evidence/v1/check_mesh_summary.json` schema. The summary gates on standard checkMesh metrics (max_skewness, max_aspect_ratio, max_non_orthogonality, boundary openness, n_cells > 0) — all of which pass on a uniform hex blockMesh with no body. The summary **does not check** that the 6 geometry-derived wall patches declared in `snappyHexMeshDict.castellatedMeshControls.refinementSurfaces.drivaer.regions` (vehicle_body / side_mirror_outboard / wheel_front_outboard / wheel_rear_outboard / mirror_edge_trim_strip / underbody_sensor_cover_thin) have any faces in the resulting polyMesh boundary file |
| Engineer symptom | case_010 v1 `check_mesh_summary.json` reads `mesh_ok=True, n_cells=4644000`; v1 REPORT.md F4 records "6 boundary patches: inlet, outlet, top, side_outboard, ground, symmetry_plane_centerline (all expected names)" — **silent about the 6 wall patches** that sHM registered but with zero faces. The `raw_tail` field reproduces checkMesh's stdout including `".*" 197268 ok (closed singly connected)` (the regex-grouped sum of all expected-domain patches), but the report-side analysis didn't pivot on the absence of named wall patches in that output. V46 sedimentation independently missed this signal |
| Root cause | The mesh-quality contract was authored for OpenFOAM's internal-geometry validity (non-orthogonality, skewness, volume positivity, face pyramid closure), which is the right contract for "is this mesh numerically usable". It is **not** the right contract for "does this mesh contain the geometry the engineer intended". The latter requires cross-referencing: (a) what regions/patches did sHM expect to populate (read snappyHexMeshDict), (b) what patches actually exist in `constant/polyMesh/boundary` post-mesh, (c) which intended patches have face_count == 0. The cross-reference layer is missing |
| Fix | (1) **Per-case immediate**: add a `geometry_patches_face_counts` field to `check_mesh_summary.json` listing each refinementSurfaces region's face count in the final polyMesh; gate `mesh_ok=False` if any expected wall patch has 0 faces. (2) **Cross-case pattern (Pillar-2)**: extract a `mesh_geometry_audit.py` advisor under DEC-V61-198 once a 2nd case surfaces the same blind spot (case_010 is first). Audit logic: parse snappyHexMeshDict for expected patch names + parse polyMesh/boundary for actual patches + report missing-or-empty intersection. (3) **Doc-only**: amend `solver_convergence_playbook.md` with new S-row "S25 candidate: checkMesh OK + zero wall faces == STL never snapped — check unit scale before re-running"  |
| Status | open — methodology gap noted; per-case fix is sub-session action; main-project advisor extraction blocked on 2nd-case Pillar-2 trigger |
| Reference case | case_010_drivaer_fastback_les v1 baseline 2026-05-08 (`~/Desktop/case_010_drivaer_fastback_les/evidence/v1/check_mesh_summary.json` mesh_ok=true; `case/log/03_snappyHexMesh.log` lines 60-71 — 6 wall patches added with 0 cells intersected; `evidence/v1/REPORT.md` §F4 silent on missing wall patches). Track C retro: `.planning/retrospectives/2026-05-13_track_c_advisor_e2e_session_1_case_010.md` |
| Lesson | A mesh-quality gate that answers "is the mesh numerically valid" is necessary but not sufficient. The complete pre-solver gate is `numerically_valid AND contains_intended_geometry`. The second clause is structurally simple (parse + cross-reference) but absent from the v1 check_mesh contract because the contract was inherited from OpenFOAM's own checkMesh, which itself has no knowledge of the engineer's intent. **Methodology pattern**: when extracting an OpenFOAM utility's output into a project artifact (check_mesh_summary.json), the project artifact must add the **intent-cross-reference** layer that OpenFOAM cannot provide. This is the same pattern as V12 ("mass conservation only checked at verdict stage — too late") and the A4 mass-balance pre-flight advisor that closes it: project layer adds intent-checks that the upstream tool doesn't know to check. **Pillar-2 trigger**: this V-row is open until a 2nd case (most likely a case where STL scale is correct but a refinementSurfaces region name typo silently produces empty patches) co-confirms the pattern; that 2nd case would justify `mesh_geometry_audit.py` extraction |

### V84 · max_skewness 4 is sHM's reject-wall, NOT a solver-instability ceiling — buoyantSimpleFoam runs stably on max_skew 6.87 / 20-skew-face industrial mesh with production-tuned schemes [case_002a F4b 2026-05-12/13]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | APU bay case_002a F4b 2026-05-12/13: v32 polyMesh (3.10M cells, max_skewness 6.875, 20 skew faces · seven-iter mesh-debug arc final state per V73-V78) + sHM `maxInternalSkewness 3 → 8` documentary relaxation + 27-patch BC contract (5 phantom patches from naming.yaml warn-and-skipped by `08_write_bcs.py`: APU_door / apu_intake / Frame_3 / Frame_6 / Plane_Outer_Surf — never landed in v32 polyMesh per V74/V75 retreat) + buoyantSimpleFoam endTime=3000 with potentialFoam `-writePhi -initialiseUBCs` warm start. Degraded physics caveat: missing `apu_intake` patch (was supposed to be mass_flow_outlet) means flow exits via `farfield_cylinder` (farfield zero-grad) instead of through the dedicated bay outlet — physics is asymmetric vs intended scenario but solver-validity is orthogonal to that. Production schemes: gradSchemes `cellLimited Gauss linear 1` (strong limiter) for U/p_rgh/h/T/k/omega; divSchemes `bounded Gauss upwind` (max robustness); laplacianSchemes `Gauss linear limited 0.5` (skewness-tolerant); fvSolution `nNonOrthogonalCorrectors 5` (potentialFoam init) / `2` (main loop · v28-stable tightening for rho-coupling); momentumPredictor on. Solver checkpoint: by step 213/3000, residuals stable — h 2.4e-5 (1 inner iter, diagonalPBiCGStab), p_rgh 1.8e-5 in 3 outer correctors × 4-5 GAMG inner iters, omega 1.4e-4, k 6.4e-4. Physics state: T 320.6-844.4 K (bounded; limitTemperature 200/1500 K limits NEVER hit), rho 0.36-1.43 kg/m³ (physical for hot bay), cumulative continuity error 4e-4 (small), limitVelocity activating on ~2.3% cells (~71k cells clamped at 150 m/s — combustor jet zone, expected). Runtime: ~16.7 s/step × 3000 = ~14h projected on single Docker `opencfd/openfoam-default:2312` container · Apple Silicon M-series host |
| Engineer symptom | After F1-F5 (V75-V78) exhausted all non-trivial mesh-tuning levers on case_002a — refinement-level coarsening, sHM dict drop, STL body deletion, per-patch refinement bumps, isotropic remeshing of simple shells, bbox shell-punch surgery — v32 still failed checkMesh PASS threshold (max_skew 6.87 > 4 default). Implicit assumption driving the seven-iteration debug arc: "max_skewness > 4 means solver will diverge / crash / produce garbage". F4b tested this assumption directly. **Result inverts the assumption**: solver runs stably with no FATAL / Floating point / Killed / Segmentation signatures and bounded residuals. The 4 threshold is sHM's iteration-acceptance criterion (when sHM rejects a candidate cell, it backs off and re-snaps), not a forecast about solver behavior on the final mesh |
| Root cause | OpenFOAM `meshQualityControls.maxSkewness 4` default is the **sHM mesh-generation accept-reject threshold**, calibrated for sHM's own iterative snap algorithm — sHM uses it to decide "is this candidate snap geometrically valid". It is conservative because sHM doesn't know what solver will run downstream nor what numerical schemes will absorb skewness. Production solver schemes — specifically (a) `cellLimited Gauss linear 1` for grads bounds the gradient at each cell face so skew-induced gradient errors can't propagate, (b) `bounded Gauss upwind` for divs treats convective transport with maximum stability, (c) `Gauss linear limited 0.5` for laplacian applies a 0.5-strength skewness correction (industry-standard tolerable-skewness handling), (d) `nNonOrthogonalCorrectors 5` in potentialFoam + `2` in main loop iterates the pressure equation enough to absorb residual non-orthogonality coupling — provide layered tolerance for max_skewness 6-8. Combined system rejects skewness as a numerical concern below ~8 with these schemes. **The 4 threshold and the 8 wall are TWO DIFFERENT THRESHOLDS**: 4 = sHM-default reject-wall (conservative, geometric); 8 = where production schemes stop absorbing (numerical, scheme-dependent). v32 at 6.87 sits comfortably between them |
| Fix | None needed — F4b empirically validates that v32 (best-of-7-iter mesh state) is solver-viable as-is. The seven-iteration debug arc V73-V78 had diminishing returns; the right exit condition was "solver runs stably" not "max_skewness < 4". **Methodology fix for future industrial cases**: when mesh quality drops below sHM's 4 default but holds above the 8 numerical wall (5-7 range), run a **5-minute solver smoke test FIRST** before pursuing more mesh-tuning interventions. If smoke-test passes (no FATAL in 50 SIMPLE iterations), accept the mesh and move on. Mesh quality is a numerical safety margin per existing Pattern 3 — but the size of the margin needed depends on scheme choice, not on sHM defaults. **Documentary edit**: sHM `meshQualityControls.maxInternalSkewness 3 → 8` carried forward in case_002a snappyHexMeshDict so future sHM runs on this geometry won't fight the threshold. **Workflow-level fix**: `09_run_solver.sh` should `make distclean-times` or check time-dir-vs-polyMesh cell-count compatibility before `startFrom latestTime` engagement — the first F4b launch failed on stale 500/U from a prior 3.16M-cell mesh run because no auto-cleanup happens between mesh-rebuild cycles (Foam `internalField` size mismatch error after potentialFoam read at time=500) |
| Status | **closed positive** — solver ran cleanly for **2689 SIMPLE iters** (ExecutionTime 37460 s = 10.4 h CPU; ClockTime 39403 s = 10.95 h wall) before user-invoked early stop via runtime `controlDict.stopAt writeNow` (originally targeted endTime=3000; halted at ~89% per user decision once stability was sufficiently demonstrated). Convergence trajectory: p_rgh initial residual climbed from 0.22 (step 100) → plateaued at **0.39-0.54 (steps 500-2689)** — flow is **statistically stable but NOT numerically converged to steady state**. T bounded throughout — Tmax 804-855 K (oscillating around ~830-840 mean, never clamped at 200/1500 K limits); Tmin 316-321 K (tight). Cumulative continuity drift: -0.027 (step 500) → -0.061 (step 2689) — ~6% mass deficit growing slowly, sign of imperfect farfield-as-outlet mass-conservation given missing dedicated `apu_intake` outlet. limitVelocity stable at 5-6% cells clamped at 150 m/s (combustor jet zone). **Diagnosis**: the case did not steady-state because (a) missing dedicated outlet patch — flow exits via `farfield_cylinder` zeroGradient, which is mass-conservative only in the integral sense not the local sense; (b) buoyant plume + combustor jet interaction is intrinsically unsteady, requires `buoyantPimpleFoam` (transient) rather than `buoyantSimpleFoam` (steady-state) for a numerically converged answer. **Mesh quality was never the bottleneck** — max_skew 6.87 / 20-skew-face mesh absorbed 2689 SIMPLE iters with zero FATAL / FPE / Killed / Segmentation signatures and bounded fields throughout. DEC-V61-198 final "Not validated · Solver execution from Claude Code Bash" item — **operationally validated**: Claude Code session drove the entire pipeline end-to-end from a single chat window (geometry ingest case_002a-history → mesh build → 7-iter mesh debug V73-V78 → STL surgery V75 F2.1 → naming.yaml audit V74 → schema drift fix V73 → BC write → potentialFoam warm-start → buoyantSimpleFoam 10.4 h live run → user-invoked clean early stop via runtime controlDict edit), no human-handoff for any step. **Open follow-ups** (NOT scope of F4b): (1) restore apu_intake patch via STL surgery on body_1 louver per V75 to recover correct outlet physics; (2) switch to `buoyantPimpleFoam` if true unsteady physics is needed, or accept time-averaged statistical-steady from a longer simpleFoam run after (1); both blocked on (1) |
| Reference case | APU bay case_002a F4b · `~/Desktop/apu-bay-ventilation/case/log/05_solver.log` (initial bz2cqh4sv background task; OpenFOAM 2312 docker container `966d36c69cba`); preceding mesh state from RESUME `.planning/case_profiles/case_002a_RESUME.md`; controlDict endTime=3000 + writeInterval=50; fvSchemes/fvSolution as described above |
| Lesson | **Two big lessons.** (1) **The implicit-threshold trap**: a seven-iteration mesh debug arc (V73-V78) was driven by the implicit assumption that sHM's `maxSkewness 4` default = solver-instability ceiling. It is not. It is a mesh-generator-internal threshold tuned for sHM's snap algorithm, calibrated conservatively because sHM doesn't know your downstream numerics. Production solver schemes (cellLimited + bounded upwind + limited laplacian + non-ortho correctors) absorb 6-8 routinely. The right diagnostic question for industrial CFD is NOT "does mesh pass checkMesh defaults" but **"does the solver run cleanly for ~50 iters with the schemes I plan to use"**. Five minutes of solver smoke beats seven hours of mesh debug. (2) **Strategic claim closure for DEC-V61-198**: Claude Code session window drove the COMPLETE workflow — geometry / mesh-build / mesh-debug / mesh-arc-sedimentation (V73-V78) / BC write / solver launch / live convergence monitoring — without leaving the chat. The four DEC-V61-198 pillars (Run-and-correct · Sediment-as-you-go · Strategic narrative coherence · Solver execution from Bash) now all have empirical session-evidence on the same case_002a. **Connecting V84 to existing patterns**: confirms Pattern 3 ("Mesh quality is a numerical safety margin") with new precision — the margin's size depends on scheme choice, not sHM defaults. Confirms Pattern 1 ("Zero IC is the universal first-iter killer") — potentialFoam warm-start was essential here too. Suggests new Pattern candidate: **"sHM mesh-quality defaults are mesh-generator-internal thresholds, not solver-stability forecasts"** (validation pending: a 2nd industrial case where production schemes absorb max_skew 6-8 → promote to confirmed pattern). **Counter row**: autonomous_governance: true (session ran the entire pipeline without external gate; F4b decision-to-launch was main-session reasoning, no DEC required for solver-launch verification per v2.3 scope-driven rule — single-test mode, no schema break, no governance change) |

### V85 · Solid-region insidePoint geometrically inside hot-fluid layer + 0.8mm plate thickness sub-cell at level (1,2) → sHM cellZone walk leaks across plates, region_hot_fluid absent from final polyMesh [backfill 2026-05-13 for case_011 v1 2026-05-09 · supersedes cross_cuts/v_series_case_011_append_2026-05-09 V48 primary attribution]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | case_011 v1 sediment 2026-05-09, surfaced retroactively by Track C advisor session 2 2026-05-13. Two compounding defects in `~/Desktop/case_011_plate_fin_compact_hx/case/system/snappyHexMeshDict`: **(a)** `castellatedMeshControls.refinementSurfaces.region_solid.insidePoint (0.090 0.060 0.013)` — z=13mm is **inside hot_layer_1 fluid**, NOT inside any solid plate. Stack-layout math from `scripts/build_cad.py`: active stack height = 2·HOT_CHANNEL_HEIGHT_MM + 1·COLD_CHANNEL_HEIGHT_MM + 4·PLATE_THICKNESS_MM = 24+16+3.2 = 43.2mm, centered in H_MM=55 with margin 5.9mm each side. Plate z-ranges: bottom_cover [5.9, 6.7] · separator_plate_1_2 [18.7, 19.5] · separator_plate_3_4 [35.5, 36.3] · top_cover [48.3, 49.1] mm. z=13mm hits hot_layer_1 [6.7, 18.7] mm. **(b)** All 4 plates are PLATE_THICKNESS_MM = 0.8 mm; sHM background_cell_size = 4mm at refinement level (1,2) → effective cell = 4/2² = 1.0 mm > 0.8 mm. Plates are sub-cell and sHM cannot resolve them as region-separating surfaces |
| Engineer symptom | `case/log/03_snappyHexMesh.log` emits a wall of `--> FOAM Warning : From void Foam::meshRefinement::findCellZoneInsideWalk(...) Cell N at (x y z) is inside cellZone region_solid from locationInMesh (0.09 0.06 0.013) but already marked as being in zone region_hot_fluid · This can happen if your surfaces are not (sufficiently) closed` for cells at z=0.0085-0.0090 m (geometrically in hot_layer_1, but the bottom_cover at z=5.9-6.7mm is too thin for sHM to seal). Cell-zone walk diagnostic: `For cellZone region_hot_fluid found point (0.09 0.06 0.045) in global region 1 out of 312 regions · region_cold_fluid in global region 17 out of 312 · region_solid in global region 1 out of 312` — all three insidePoints walk into the same fragmented connected-component graph because the 0.8mm plates and 1.0mm hot-side fins both fail to seal. `splitMeshRegions -cellZones -overwrite` output (`case/log/04_splitMeshRegions.log`): **only 3 regions emit polyMesh — domain0 (734,854 cells, no cellZone), region_solid (240,100 cells), region_cold_fluid (5,664 cells); region_hot_fluid is silently absent**. `evidence/v1/mesh_summary.json` records `regions.region_hot_fluid.present = false`. cold_fluid retains only 3% of geometric-expected cell count (5,664 vs ~173k = 36×16×120×2.5 mm³ at 1mm cell), most absorbed into the `domain0` orphan |
| Root cause | Two distinct contributing defects compound. **(a) Configuration bug**: solid insidePoint authored against an earlier sizing-convergence layout that assumed bottom_cover starts at z=0; build_cad's `build_stack_layout` centers the stack with `(H_MM - active_height) / 2` margin (5.9mm), so all plate z-ranges shift up by 5.9mm. The dict was not re-emitted to track the build's geometry. **(b) Refinement-vs-thickness physics**: at refinement level (1,2), effective cell = 4mm/2² = 1.0mm. Plate thickness 0.8mm and hot-side fin thickness 1.0mm are both below the V10/V23/V30/V37 thin-wall threshold (need `effective_cell ≤ thickness / 2` for sHM seal). `thin_wall_advisor` PRE-MESH ran only on `cold_fin_rear_third` (D8 patch · severity=critical · recommended_level_max=4) — it was **NOT run on plates** because plate-bounding surfaces were not declared as named patches with thickness inputs. The advisor coverage gap is the systemic finding: when a multi-region CHT geometry exposes thin-wall surfaces that **bound regions** (not just patches with thermal constraints), thin_wall_advisor must run on them too. **Pillar-2 cross-application of V83**: the audit JSON `mesh_summary.json` verdict `PARTIAL_FRAGMENTED` with `interpretation` calling cold_fluid "split cleanly" downplays a missing expected region — same blind-spot class as V83 ("mesh_ok=true despite zero-face patches") but at a different verdict surface (multi-region split) |
| Fix | Per-case sub-session action (case_011 v2 already planned per `case_profiles/case_011_plate_fin_compact_hx.md` v2 row): **(1)** Reposition `refinementSurfaces.region_solid.insidePoint` to the centroid of an actual plate cell — recommended (0.090, 0.060, 0.0063) for bottom_cover or (0.090, 0.060, 0.0191) for separator_plate_1_2. **(2)** Bump refinement on plate-bearing solid surfaces to level (3, 4) (effective cell 0.25mm = 3.2 cells per 0.8mm plate thickness — safe per V10 / S22 rule). The v1 plan only bumps the cold_fin_rear_third patch; the plate surfaces also need the bump or they cannot seal. **(3)** Update `scripts/02_scaffold_case.py` (or whichever step emits snappyHexMeshDict) to compute insidePoint coordinates from the build-time stack layout, not from a hardcoded value tied to an earlier sizing iteration. **Cross-case methodology fix (Pillar-2 candidate)**: extend `thin_wall_advisor` invocation to enumerate ALL region-bounding surfaces (plates between fluid regions, not just thermally-active fins), with thickness inputs derived from the build_cad parts manifest. Currently the advisor is invoked manually per known-thin patch by the case-author; the candidate pattern is **auto-enumeration over the parts manifest** so the author doesn't have to remember to check plates separately from fins |
| Status | **fix-verified · 1 case (case_011 v4 · 2026-05-13)**. V85's two-defect fix path landed via case_011 v2 sub-session (2026-05-13). Defect (a) closed: solid `insidePoint (0.090 0.060 0.013)` → `(0.090 0.060 0.0191)` (centroid of `separator_plate_1_2`). Defect (b) closed: `region_solid.level (1 2)` → `(3 4)` (effective cell 0.25mm, 3.2 cells per 0.8mm plate). Post-fix v4 sHM (5140s, 15.20M cells, 1328 illegal faces) + `splitMeshRegions` produces 6 mesh regions with **region_hot_fluid populated** (3,339,756 cells, volume 3.07×10⁻⁴ m³, retention 142% of analytic channel volume — over-100% because mesh extends into hot manifolds beyond envelope). cellZone walk: region_hot_fluid now in global region 1 of 271 (single connected component, vs v1's 1 of 312 fragmented). region_solid in distinct global region 2 (vs v1's 1 — co-located with hot, confirming v1's missing seal). Verification evidence in `~/Desktop/case_011_plate_fin_compact_hx/evidence/v2/REPORT.md` (§2 v1↔v4 diagnostic table; §3 narrow §9 criterion verification). **Cross-case advisor-enumeration extension still deferred** to N≥2 plate-class case (Pillar-2 trigger); case_002b (single-stream CHT with shell plates) is the highest-probability next candidate. **Verification surfaces V89 + V90** (this session, see corresponding rows): the v2 attempt revealed two NEW failure modes that V85 did not name — V89 (fluid-region insidePoint authored in fin space) and V90 (modern locationsInMesh syntax incompatible with separate-STL multi-region). Both are in the dict-orchestration family but distinct from V85's solid-insidePoint pattern. Sub-DEC: `2026-05-13_v61_198_sub_case_011_v2_fix_verification.md` |
| Reference case | case_011_plate_fin_compact_hx v1 baseline 2026-05-09 (`~/Desktop/case_011_plate_fin_compact_hx/case/log/03_snappyHexMesh.log` lines 175-228 for cellZone-walk FOAM Warnings, lines 320-369 for the conflict-walk reprise after baffle introduction; `case/log/04_splitMeshRegions.log` lines 38-58 showing 3-region output without region_hot_fluid; `case/system/snappyHexMeshDict` lines 49-53 for the misplaced solid insidePoint; `evidence/v1/mesh_summary.json` `regions.region_hot_fluid.present=false` + shm_cellzone_walk showing 312 global components; `scripts/build_cad.py:98-136` `build_stack_layout` for the 5.9mm margin math). Track C retro: `.planning/retrospectives/2026-05-13_track_c_advisor_e2e_session_2_case_011.md` |
| Lesson | When a multi-region CHT CAD is built parametrically with `(H_envelope - active_stack_height) / 2` margin centering, **any hardcoded coordinate in the meshing dict that references an absolute z is fragile across re-sizes**. The 5.9mm margin was an output of `build_stack_layout` math, not an input — the snappyHexMeshDict was authored against a different layout assumption and never reconciled. **Methodology pattern**: when CAD generation and mesh configuration are in separate scripts, the mesh script must derive its coordinates from the CAD build's emitted layout (via parts manifest), not from a side-channel earlier-iteration value. **Cross-case methodology pattern**: `thin_wall_advisor` coverage today is patch-centric (the case-author lists the patches they think are at risk). The case_011 failure mode is that **region-bounding surfaces (plates) are at-risk too**, and the case-author didn't enumerate them. Either the advisor needs to auto-enumerate from the parts manifest (advisor-side fix), or the case-author intake template needs an explicit `region_bounding_surfaces` field (template-side fix). **Connecting V85 to existing patterns**: this is a Pattern 4 candidate ("Configuration-time checks beat verdict-time checks") — an `insidePoint vs build_stack_layout consistency` pre-check would catch defect (a) before sHM spends 871 seconds producing a fragmented mesh; a parts-manifest-driven thin-wall enumerator would catch (b). **Cross-application of V83**: V83 said "checkMesh OK + zero faces on expected patches = silent acceptance"; V85 case adds the multi-region analog: `mesh_summary.json verdict=PARTIAL_FRAGMENTED with regions[name].present=false = silent acceptance of a missing region`. The mesh-summary contract needs the same intent-cross-reference layer that V83 prescribes for check_mesh_summary — gate `verdict=FAIL` (not PARTIAL) whenever `any(expected_region not present)`. **Inheritance — what V47-V50 (cross_cuts draft) missed**: the case_011 sub-session sediment to `.planning/cross_cuts/v_series_case_011_append_2026-05-09.md` (draft V47 BC bookkeeping, draft V48 sHM snap struggle, draft V49 A2 placeholder, draft V50 thin_wall PASS) correctly identified the fin-snap struggle (draft V48) but attributed it to "BASE_FIN_THICKNESS_MM = 1.0 mm at level (1,2)" only — it did NOT name the plate-thickness sub-cell failure or the misplaced solid insidePoint as the **load-bearing cause for region_hot_fluid being absent from the final polyMesh**. The cross_cuts draft V47-V50 never elevated to the main corpus; main corpus V47-V51 instead document case_015 findings. This V85 backfill closes the case_011 v1 sediment gap |

### V86 · `surfaceFeatureExtract` writes .eMesh files but `snappyHexMeshDict.castellatedMeshControls.features ()` is empty list → upstream stage output orphaned; `multiRegionFeatureSnap true` + `implicitFeatureSnap true` has nothing to act on [case_011 v1 2026-05-09]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | case_011 v1 sediment 2026-05-09. `~/Desktop/case_011_plate_fin_compact_hx/case/system/snappyHexMeshDict`: `castellatedMeshControls { features (); }` is declared empty. `snapControls`: `nFeatureSnapIter 10; implicitFeatureSnap true; explicitFeatureSnap false; multiRegionFeatureSnap true`. The pipeline runs `02_surfaceFeatureExtract` which successfully writes 3 .eMesh files: `constant/triSurface/region_hot_fluid.eMesh` (504 edges · 264 external + 240 internal), `region_cold_fluid.eMesh` (7968 edges · 600 external + 7368 internal), `region_solid.eMesh` (1468 edges · 776 external + 692 internal). The `.eMesh` files are present on disk and the surfaceFeatureExtract log records successful writes — but sHM never references them because the `features ()` list is empty |
| Engineer symptom | sHM log shows the symptom but it is **non-FATAL and undocumented**: line 46 reads `Reading features. Read features in = 0 s` — zero time because zero features. Subsequent feature-driven refinement counters all report zero: `Marked for refinement due to distance to explicit features : 0 cells` across all 3 transition iterations. With `explicitFeatureSnap false` and an empty features list, the sHM relies entirely on implicit feature snap (`resolveFeatureAngle 30°`) and the snap-controls `multiRegionFeatureSnap true` setting — but multiRegionFeatureSnap is **a parameter that controls how multi-region feature edges are handled when they exist**, not a substitute for declaring them. Final snap quality on the sub-cell-plate geometry was `Did not successfully snap mesh. Continuing to snap to resolve easy surfaces but the resulting mesh will not satisfy your quality constraints` + 86 illegal faces. Stage 02 ran for 0.1 s of useful work that downstream stage 03 silently ignored |
| Root cause | Two intersecting design intents both broken. **(a) Pipeline orchestration gap**: the case scaffolding (`scripts/02_scaffold_case.py`) emits `surfaceFeatureExtractDict` (causing stage 02 to run) AND emits `snappyHexMeshDict` with an empty `features ()` block. Stage 02's outputs are produced but not consumed. Either stage 02 should not run, OR stage 03 should reference the .eMesh files via `features ( { file "region_hot_fluid.eMesh"; level 2; } { file "region_cold_fluid.eMesh"; level 2; } { file "region_solid.eMesh"; level 3; } )`. **(b) Snap-controls semantic confusion**: `multiRegionFeatureSnap true` + `features ()` empty + `explicitFeatureSnap false` is **internally inconsistent**. multiRegionFeatureSnap reconciles feature edges that span multiple cellZones — it cannot reconcile what is never declared. Author appears to have intended "snap to features automatically" but the OpenFOAM idiom for that is `implicitFeatureSnap true` alone (which is on); `multiRegionFeatureSnap` is an orthogonal switch that needs explicit features to do its job. Setting it `true` with no features is misleading but not harmful — it is dead config |
| Fix | Per-case sub-session action: **(1)** wire stage 02's .eMesh outputs into stage 03's `features` block, with per-region snap level: `features ( { file "region_hot_fluid.eMesh"; level 2; } { file "region_cold_fluid.eMesh"; level 2; } { file "region_solid.eMesh"; level 3; } )` — level matches the refinementSurfaces level for that region. **(2)** Switch `explicitFeatureSnap` to `true` once features are wired, so the 10 `nFeatureSnapIter` iterations actually iterate over feature points. **(3)** Either keep `multiRegionFeatureSnap true` (with features wired, it now does its job for region interfaces — required for chtMR multi-region meshing of plate-fin geometry) OR set it `false` if the per-region snap is sufficient. **Cross-case methodology fix (Pillar-2 candidate)**: a `shm_dict_validator` advisor (already drafted in roadmap per M-A8) should warn when `castellatedMeshControls.features` is empty but `.eMesh` files exist in `constant/triSurface/`, OR when `multiRegionFeatureSnap true` co-occurs with empty features list. Two-line static check |
| Status | **[VALIDATED] 2026-05-14** — A8 `shm_dict_validator` landed (DEC-V61-198-sub-A8). `ui/backend/services/geometry_ingest/shm_dict_validator.py` ships with 9-test suite covering V52 typo class + V86 orphan class; `test_v86_features_list_orphan_regression_case_011` pins the case_011 v1 ground truth (3 .eMesh files in `available_emeshes` + empty `castellatedMeshControls.features ()` → critical `orphaned_emesh_feature` finding with rewire suggestion). Promotion gate met by V52 (case_012 typo) + V86 (case_011 orphan) cross-topology pair per V25→A2-v2 convention. Previously: fix-verified · 1 case (case_011 v4 · 2026-05-13) — case_011 v2 sub-session wired `features ( { file "region_hot_fluid.eMesh"; level 2; } { file "region_cold_fluid.eMesh"; level 2; } { file "region_solid.eMesh"; level 2; } )`; post-fix v4 sHM `Read features in` reports non-zero edges processed. Sub-DECs: `2026-05-13_v61_198_sub_case_011_v2_fix_verification.md` (case-local fix) + `2026-05-14_v61_198_sub_a8_shm_dict_validator.md` (cross-case validator) |
| Reference case | case_011_plate_fin_compact_hx v1 baseline 2026-05-09 (`case/system/snappyHexMeshDict` lines 26 `features ();` + lines 65-73 `snapControls` block; `case/log/02_surfaceFeatureExtract.log` lines 28-186 documenting successful .eMesh writes for all 3 regions; `case/log/03_snappyHexMesh.log` line 46 `Read features in = 0 s` confirming non-use; `case/constant/triSurface/region_*.eMesh` files present on disk). Track C retro: `.planning/retrospectives/2026-05-13_track_c_advisor_e2e_session_2_case_011.md` |
| Lesson | OpenFOAM's multi-stage meshing pipeline is **silent on stage-to-stage data flow**: each utility reads its own dictionary, writes its own outputs, and trusts the user to wire the next stage's inputs to match. `surfaceFeatureExtract` does not know whether sHM will reference its .eMesh; sHM does not warn when an .eMesh exists in `constant/triSurface/` but isn't in its features list. The orchestration gap is a **Pattern 4 candidate** (Configuration-time checks beat verdict-time checks): a one-shot `shm_dict_validator` that cross-references stage 02 outputs against stage 03 features-list would close the gap at config-time. **Methodology pattern**: when the engineer follows the official "extract features then snap" workflow, the validator should confirm the link. Currently the only feedback channel is the sHM log's `Read features in = 0 s` line — which is easy to miss in a 122k-token log. **Inheritance — what case_011 cross_cuts draft V47-V50 missed**: the draft V-rows focused on the multi-stream BC bookkeeping and the thin-wall fin advisor confirmation. The features-list orphaning was a separate orchestration class of defect — neither flagged by thin_wall_advisor (which is patch-thickness-centric) nor by the BC bookkeeping checklist. **Connecting V86 to existing patterns**: same family as V47 (minMedialAxisAngle typo silently broke layer addition · "OF v2312 raises FATAL but older OF silently rejected"), V81 (Codex CAD emitted inlet/outlet as solids → sHM treated as walls · silent sealed-room), V58/V62 (build_domain_patches construction silently overlapped with flat domain). The thread is **"the OpenFOAM utility accepts the input as syntactically valid and proceeds; the failure surfaces only in a downstream metric that the author wasn't watching"**. Pattern 4 closure: configuration-time validators per dict |

### V87 · D7 second injection completes A4 promotion gate — case_013 CAM blade-3 LE rotated 22° around chord axis, measured 21.979° via tilt-from-XY-plane signature [case_013 v1 2026-05-13 · closed 2026-05-13 by DEC-V61-198-sub-A4]

| field | value |
|---|---|
