| Surface | case_013_centrifugal_pump_cavitating v1 sediment 2026-05-13. `inputs/cad_codex_v1.step` injects D7 on `blade_3`: leading-edge chunk (20mm × 14mm × blade-height + 4mm cut_box) rotated 22° around the LE chord axis (axis in XY plane). Verification via `scripts/check_face_normal.py` against a `D7_ROTATION_DEG=0` baseline STEP (`inputs/cad_codex_v1_baseline.step`). Measurement: blade_3 in injected geometry has 55 STEP faces vs 48 in baseline (Δ+7 faces from cut+fuse); bbox z extends to z=20.1mm vs baseline 19.5mm (rotated chunk corners protrude); LE-band side-face max tilt-from-XY-plane = 21.979° (expected 22.000°, delta −0.021°, tolerance 4°, PASS). Method: baseline blade extrusion has all side-face normals in XY plane (n_z=0); D7 rotation around an XY-plane chord axis tilts the rotated chunk's side faces into n_z ≠ 0 with peak tilt ≈ rotation angle. `evidence/v1/defect_verification_d7.json` records full topological + tilt signature |
| Engineer symptom | Identical advisor-stack-scope-gap symptom as V79 (case_012 D7 first injection): no LANDED advisor detects "a face/body has been rotated from its intended orientation". The geometry passes A1 (CAD ingest), A2 (shared-interface detection — no impact on interface topology), A3 (health check — geometry remains watertight), thin_wall_advisor (still 4-6mm sides). Downstream effect on cavitating pump physics: rotated LE alters tip-leakage incidence angle, biasing the suction-side cavitation onset location and predicted NPSH_3% margin — but only visible after running cavitatingFoam (out of v1 scope). **case_013 differs from case_012 D7 in two respects**: (a) rotation axis is in the XY plane (chord axis) not the Z axis (vane mounting axis) — produces a different geometric signature in the STEP; (b) injection is on a sub-feature (LE chunk fused back into blade body via .cut() + .rotate() + .fuse()) rather than a whole-body component rotation. Both produce the same advisor-detection gap |
| Root cause | Same root cause as V79: the advisor stack treats CAD-exported geometry as authoritative for orientation; "rotated component" or "rotated sub-feature" is an assembly-instruction-violation class of defect that no current advisor module covers. The closest existing primitive is `find_face_facing_target` (A2's helper), which consumes orientation as input but does not validate it as a defect signature. **case_013 v1 confirms generalizability across rotation-axis topology**: case_012's whole-vane rotation around Z and case_013's LE-chunk rotation around an XY-axis both produce the same "advisor sees nothing wrong" symptom even though the geometric signatures are different. The defect class is **face-normal-orientation deviation from intended/sibling-consensus**, regardless of which axis the rotation occurred around |
| Fix | **A4 promotion gate now met** (≥ 2 cross-topology cases with manual-verified D7 injection per `.planning/methodology/advisor_candidates_a4_a8.md` promotion-gate spec): case_012 (V79, whole-vane Z-axis rotation, FreeCAD principal-normal dot-product method) + case_013 (V87, LE-chunk XY-axis rotation, FreeCAD tilt-from-XY-plane method). Both produce manual measurements that match design intent (V79: 38.000° exact; V87: 21.979° vs 22.000° design within 0.021° tolerance). **A4 advisor land-ready** — research deliverable already drafted at `.planning/patches/draft_a4_face_orientation_2026-05-13.md` (commit `615dacb`). Implementation path: lands as `ui/backend/services/geometry_ingest/face_orientation_advisor.py` per draft spec, with case_012 + case_013 D7 STEP files becoming the canonical regression test inputs. **Cross-case methodology**: the **measurement method** depends on rotation axis topology — Z-axis rotations need principal-normal dot-product (case_012 pattern), XY-axis rotations need tilt-from-XY-plane (case_013 pattern). The productized advisor must auto-select method based on declared rotation_axis in parts_manifest (or attempt both and use the cleaner signal). Sub-session also exercised D1 (tip clearance 0.8mm on blade_5 vs 0.5mm baseline) — A2-v2 should detect, but unrelated to A4 promotion |
| Status | **[VALIDATED] 2026-05-13** — A4 `face_orientation_advisor` landed (DEC-V61-198-sub-A4). `ui/backend/services/geometry_ingest/face_orientation_advisor.py` ships with 9-test suite covering both rotation topologies; `test_case_013_blade_3_regression` pins the V87 21.979° measurement (max-tilt face normal `[-0.9017, -0.2166, 0.3743]` from `defect_verification_d7.json`, intended = XY-projection re-normalized, tolerance 4° → critical). Cross-case methodology insight resolved: rather than auto-selecting measurement method by `rotation_axis` (originally proposed), the advisor relies on the caller pre-extracting `actual_face_normal` with the topology-appropriate method (Z-axis: principal-normal dot-product; XY-axis: tilt-from-XY-plane signature) — the productized `abs(dot)` comparison is topology-agnostic once both vectors are in hand. `autonomous_governance: true` (sub-DEC closure derivable from V79 + V87 evidence pair) |
| Reference case | case_013_centrifugal_pump_cavitating v1 2026-05-13 (`~/Desktop/case_013_centrifugal_pump_cavitating/scripts/check_face_normal.py` env-var-driven freecadcmd entry point; `inputs/cad_codex_v1.step` injected geometry; `inputs/cad_codex_v1_baseline.step` D7=0 anchor; `evidence/v1/defect_verification_d7.json` 21.979° measurement record; `inputs/defect_manifest.yaml` D7 block lines 16-25; `scripts/build_cad.py:make_blade_solid` lines 215-256 for D7 rotation logic). Parallel evidence: case_012 V79 entry above |
| Lesson | Two-case cross-topology evidence pattern played out faster than V79 expected (case_013 dispatched 2026-05-08, sedimented 2026-05-13 = 5 days). The key generalization insight: **the same defect class can have geometrically distinct verification methods depending on rotation-axis topology**, but the engineering symptom (silent flow-pattern asymmetry, no advisor catches it) is invariant. The A4 advisor spec must handle both axis topologies; case_012 + case_013 together define the verification-method test matrix the implementation must satisfy. **Methodology pattern**: when a defect class has multiple geometric realizations (rotation around different axis classes), the cross-topology gate validates the *advisor's generalization capability* not just *the defect class exists*. **Connecting V87 to existing patterns**: parallel to V25 (A2 v1 placeholder limitation surfaced over 10 cross-topology cases → A2-v2 with `inter_face_gap_mm` field-validation), V79 was a single-case probe and V87 is the cross-topology confirmation. The **2-case promotion gate** vs the **10-case overdetermined-evidence gate** (A2-v2) reflects defect-class detection difficulty: A4 is a low-hanging fruit (face-normal computation is well-defined) while A2-v2 needed compounded evidence because the placeholder-vs-real distinction was subtle. **Counter row**: autonomous_governance: true (cross-topology evidence pair met without main-session intervention; promotion is mechanically derivable from V79 + V87) |

### V88 · MRF (rotating-machinery) setup has zero pre-mesh advisor coverage — three independent sub-mechanisms surface in case_004 v1 [methodology gap · case_004 v1 2026-05-08 · 1 case · open · backfill 2026-05-13 by Track C session 3]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | case_004_nrel_phase_vi_mrf v1 advisor-validation 2026-05-08 (paused per Pattern 5 v1 simplification, mesh + solver deferred to v2). v1 ships three NEW infrastructure artifacts totaling ~315 LOC: `templates/constant/MRFProperties.j2` (32 lines), `scripts/08b_write_mrf.py` (60 LOC writer), `scripts/07b_audit_mrf.py` (245 LOC post-mesh audit). Emitted `case/constant/MRFProperties` is the only OpenFOAM artifact in v1 case/. Sub-session correctly recognized rotating-machinery as new numerics class and authored case-local infra; structural blind spot is what was NOT authored alongside |
| Engineer symptom | Three independent gaps in MRF advisor coverage surface from case_004 v1 substrate alone: (**a · NEW-infrastructure regression-test gap**) `07b_audit_mrf.py` regex parses cellZones ASCII via `r'(\w+)\s*\{\s*type\s+cellZone\s*;\s*cellLabels\s+List<label>\s*(\d+)\s*\('` (lines 70-76) and boundary via similar pattern (lines 87-95); the "rotating wall enclosed by zone bbox" heuristic on lines 200-220 is bbox-only and silently returns OK on near-misses at the snap boundary. No unit test, no frozen-reference fixture against any OpenFOAM version. `08b_write_mrf.py` writer ships without a diff-vs-frozen-reference pytest. Dead infrastructure: written 2026-05-08, still unexercised against real polyMesh 2026-05-13 = 5+ days at risk. v2 will be the first invocation. (**b · MRF zone-clearance physics gap**) `build_cad.py` line 56: `ROTATING_ZONE_RADIUS_MM = 1.12 * ROTOR_RADIUS_MM` = 5632.48 mm for R=5029 mm = only 12% radial clearance over blade tip. Wind-turbine MRF best practice = 1.5-2.0 × R clearance to place MRF↔stationary interface in low-vorticity wake (outside tip-vortex roll-up region 1.1-1.4 × R). At 1.12 × R the frozen-rotor interface sits inside the tip-vortex region; produces non-physical step discontinuities in axial/tangential velocity at the boundary; predicted Ct/Cq under-resolves tip losses by ~3-8%. (**c · nonRotatingPatches geometric isolation invariant**) `case/constant/MRFProperties` emits `nonRotatingPatches ()` empty. By geometric isolation (rotating_cellzone x ∈ [-1100,+700] mm; all stationary hardware nacelle_body / tower_body / nacelle_service_cover / yaw_sensor_shim sit at x > 700 mm), this is **currently safe** — but invariant is implicit. No advisor / no 08b writer / no 07b audit asserts `max(stationary.bbox.x) < min(zone.bbox.x) OR min(stationary.bbox.x) > max(zone.bbox.x)`. If v2 widens zone (per gap-b fix) without re-positioning stationary hardware, MRF silently auto-rotates stationary patches; symptom = anomalous near-stationary-wall force contributions |
| Root cause | Rotating-machinery is the only numerics class in the 16-case v6.2 roster (Pattern 6 root) lacking pre-mesh advisor coverage. case_004 is the only MRF case to date. The advisor stack (A1-A8 LANDED + A9-A10 candidates) covers (1) CAD ingest (A1/A4/A7), (2) defect detection (A2/A3), (3) sHM/dict orchestration (A5/A8 candidates), (4) post-processing (A6 HVAC candidate). **No advisor checks MRF-specific invariants**: zone-clearance vs rotor R, stationary-walls-outside-zone-bbox, omega-axis-vs-blade-orientation consistency, ramp-up policy for transient AMI fallback. Each is a small check (10-30 LOC) but the class is uncovered. case_004 v1 author correctly authored case-local audit but **as unrun-in-v1 dead-infra artifact**; structural fix is to ship a frozen-reference pytest pair alongside any NEW infra. **Cross-application to V83 audit-verdict semantics**: 07b_audit_mrf.py's bbox-only "rotating wall enclosed by zone" heuristic is the same permissive-verdict pattern V83 documents for `mesh_ok` in check_mesh_summary.json (case_010) and session 2 surfaced for `PARTIAL_FRAGMENTED` in mesh_summary.json (case_011). V88-a is the **3rd cross-application of V83 across 3 distinct audit surfaces in 3 Track C sessions** → V83 methodology gap overdetermined for Pillar-2 advisor extraction (`audit_verdict_semantics_advisor`) |
| Fix | (1) **Case-local v2 actions** (case_004 v2 sub-session): (i) increase `ROTATING_ZONE_RADIUS_MM` from 5632 → 7500-10000 mm (1.5-2.0 × R clearance); rebuild STEP + re-extract STL + re-emit parts_manifest `radius_mm`. (ii) Add `validate_mrf_geometry()` in 02_verify_defects.py OR new `02b_audit_mrf_geometry.py`: read parts_manifest + STEP bboxes; assert stationary-hardware-bbox-x disjoint from rotating_cellzone-bbox-x; fail-fast on violation. ~30 LOC. (iii) Add pytest fixture for `07b_audit_mrf.py`: synthesize a 50-line cellZones ASCII + boundary fragment in `tests/` (in case_004 or upstream main repo), assert parser returns expected (n_cells, patch_count) tuples; pin against OpenFOAM 2312 explicitly. (iv) Add pytest fixture for `08b_write_mrf.py`: diff emitted MRFProperties against a frozen reference file. Both fixtures ~30 LOC each. (v) Run 07b_audit_mrf.py in v2 against actual polyMesh; document any regex / heuristic bugs in V88 follow-up entry. (2) **Pillar-2 extraction candidate (A9 D-class, post-2nd-MRF-case)**: extract `ui/backend/services/mesh_quality/mrf_setup_advisor.py` covering zone-clearance check vs rotor R (sub-mechanism b), stationary-walls-outside-zone-bbox invariant (sub-mechanism c), frozen-reference regression tests for 07b/08b (sub-mechanism a). Wait for 2nd MRF case to confirm class-wide vs case-specific — 16-case roster has no other current MRF (case_015 chtMR-LES has cellZones for CHT not rotation). (3) **Cross-cut methodology amendment**: any sub-session that AUTHORS NEW INFRASTRUCTURE during v1 (Jinja2 template + writer + audit) must (a) ship a unit test for the writer against a frozen reference output AND (b) ship a unit test for the audit against a frozen reference polyMesh fragment. Without (a)+(b) the infra accumulates silent regression debt until a 2nd case forces exercise. Add to `codex_case_design_protocol.md` |
| Reference case | case_004_nrel_phase_vi_mrf v1 advisor-validation 2026-05-08 (`~/Desktop/case_004_nrel_phase_vi_mrf/evidence/v1_20260508T093722/REPORT.md` Step 4-5; `case/constant/MRFProperties` lines 18-28; `scripts/07b_audit_mrf.py` lines 42-106 regex parsers + lines 199-221 enclosure heuristic; `scripts/08b_write_mrf.py` lines 30-58; `templates/constant/MRFProperties.j2` 32 lines; `scripts/build_cad.py` line 56 `ROTATING_ZONE_RADIUS_MM = 1.12 * ROTOR_RADIUS_MM`; `config/case.yaml` lines 80-86 mrf.zones block; `inputs/parts_manifest.yaml` lines 30-38 rotating_cellzone block); cross-link to V83 (case_010 mesh_ok blind-spot pattern); cross-link to session 2 §3 (case_011 PARTIAL_FRAGMENTED V83 cross-application); cross-link to V22/V23/V24 (case_004 advisor PASSes — separate findings not affecting V88) |

Cross-class observation: V83 pattern in 3rd cross-application surface (3 distinct audit-script verdict heuristics across 3 Track C sessions) — `audit_verdict_semantics_advisor` Pillar-2 trigger now overdetermined |
| Lesson | When a sub-session AUTHORS NEW INFRASTRUCTURE during v1 (Jinja2 template + writer + audit script), the natural pause point is "v1 ships infra + v2 exercises it" — but without explicit test coverage, the infra carries silent regression risk. case_004 v1 paused at "infra written, MRFProperties emitted, 07b not yet exercised" — perfectly legitimate Pattern 5 v1 simplification, BUT absence of regression tests against frozen reference outputs makes v2 the first surfacing of any bugs. **Cross-case methodology distinction**: "v1 simplification pause" (legitimate · per Pattern 5) vs "v1 dead-infra pause" (carries debt). The fix is a 20-LOC frozen-reference pytest pair, not a workflow change. **A9 candidate registered**: `mrf_setup_advisor.py` (zone clearance + stationary-wall isolation + frozen-reference regression tests for 07b/08b) — defer to 2nd MRF case. **Pillar-2 reinforcement**: 3 cross-applications of V83 across 3 Track C sessions on 3 distinct audit-script verdict semantics (check_mesh_summary mesh_ok, mesh_summary PARTIAL_FRAGMENTED verdict, mrf_audit bbox-only enclosure heuristic) → audit-verdict-semantics is class-wide methodology gap, no longer a case-specific quirk; ready for cross-cutting `audit_verdict_semantics_advisor` sub-DEC. **Sediment author**: Claude Code Opus 4.7 (1M context) · Track C session 3 retro · 2026-05-13 |

## Cross-cutting patterns observed

### Pattern 1 — Zero IC is the universal first-iter killer

V3, V6, V11 are all variants of "first iter has no flow field, derived
quantities go to garbage". `potentialFoam -writePhi` warm start is
the single highest-leverage fix; main project should make it the
default for any compressible / buoyant / multi-BC case.

### Pattern 2 — Preconditioner choice trumps solver choice

V4, V5 both resolve by changing preconditioner, not solver. The
default OpenFOAM `DIC` / `GAMG` choices are tuned for academic
benchmark cases; industrial sHM meshes need `diagonal` /
`PBiCGStab` instead. Main project default fvSolution should reflect
this.

### Pattern 3 — Mesh quality is a numerical safety margin

V8 underlies V3-V7 in many real cases. Tightening
`meshQualityControls` is preventive medicine; loose controls let
sHM accept cells that contaminate everything downstream. A3
(geometry surgery) attacks this at the geometry source.

### Pattern 4 — Configuration-time checks beat verdict-time checks

V12 (mass conservation pre-flight) is the canonical case. Same
pattern likely applies to: BC type ↔ patch type consistency,
turbulence model ↔ wall function compatibility, solver ↔ schemes
compatibility. Each of these can fail at runtime but is detectable
at configuration time.

### Pattern 5 — v1 simplification is not a failure mode

V3 (laminar fallback), V6 (pressure BC instead of mass-flow), V13
(pseudo-steady) are all "the production physics did not work; the
v1 simplified version did". This is the correct engineering
sequence: get a working baseline, then layer complexity. Tooling
should encourage this, not penalize it.

### Pattern 6 — V-findings inherit across solver families when fluid-internal numerics are shared (added V15 · 2026-05-07)

V15 demonstrates that V5, V6, V7 (originally surfaced under
buoyantSimpleFoam) reappear unchanged in chtMultiRegionSimpleFoam,
because both solvers wrap the same compressible-buoyant-RANS
fluid-side numerics. Going forward:

- Index V-findings by **fluid-internal numerics class**
  (compressible-buoyant-RANS, incompressible-RANS, compressible-
  shock-density-based, multiphase-VOF, etc.), not by solver name
- A new industrial case in solver class X should pre-emptively
  inherit all V-findings whose `numerics_class` matches X's
  fluid-side numerics
- Genuinely new V-findings emerge only from surfaces that **did
  not exist** in the parent class — e.g. multi-region pairing,
  cellZone definition, periodic boundaries — not from fluid
  internals

The corpus loader (M6 prerequisite) should expose this inheritance
explicitly so AI Diagnose can suggest "your case is class
compressible-buoyant-RANS, here are the 6 V-findings that apply
even though you're using a different solver family".

## Corpus sync arrears (synced through V86 as of 2026-05-13)

`docs/openfoam_corpus/industrial_solver_findings_v_series.md` (consumed by
`ui/backend/services/ai_advisor/corpus_loader.py`, which feeds the N6
`/ai-review` and `/ai-diagnose` routes) is the **runtime advisor copy** of
this file. The two are kept in sync.

**Status 2026-05-13 (Track C session 2 update)**: runtime copy is **synced through V86**. V85+V86 landed in `docs/openfoam_corpus/industrial_solver_findings_v_series.md` in the same commit as this methodology update, per pre-commit `check_corpus_sync.py` hook (drift-prevention option 1, landed 2026-05-13 commit `d53afbc`). Verification: both files contain `### V85` and `### V86` headers; no drift trailer needed.

**Prior status 2026-05-14**: runtime copy was **synced through V84**. The V58..V84 backfill (27 V-rows) + V46 2026-05-13 amendment landed in `docs/openfoam_corpus/industrial_solver_findings_v_series.md` that session, closing the 27-row arrears originally surfaced by Codex review on `e56f160..e89a88f` (P2 finding · "patch does not make case_010 lessons available to advisor routes"). Verification: `test_n6_1_corpus_loader.py` 25/25 PASS · `test_ai_advisor_contract.py` PASS · `test_n6_5_offline_fallback.py` PASS · ad-hoc `find_relevant("STL scale", top_k=3)` returns V82 as the top hit.

**Drift-prevention proposal** (sediment hardening, NOT scope of this
sync commit):

The 27-row arrears built up because the runtime copy is hand-synced
after sediment lands here; nothing fails fast when the two diverge.
Three drift-prevention options, in increasing rigor:

1. **Lightweight pre-commit hook** — when this file changes, fail the
   commit if `docs/openfoam_corpus/industrial_solver_findings_v_series.md`
   was not touched in the same commit (or carry an explicit
   `Corpus-sync-defer: <reason>` trailer to opt out for trailer-only
   commits). Cheap, surfaces drift at the latest possible safe point.
2. **Per-session sediment-end check** — at session close, diff `^### V`
   header counts between methodology and runtime copy; if the methodology
   is ahead by ≥1 V-row, emit a sediment-end warning routing to a
   `/corpus-sync` flow. Less mechanical but easier to ignore.
3. **Make the runtime copy a generated artifact** — derive
   `docs/openfoam_corpus/industrial_solver_findings_v_series.md` from
   methodology via a content-stripping transform (drop the Quick-lookup
   index + governance frontmatter; keep V-rows + Cross-cutting + How-to-add).
   Eliminates drift structurally but requires careful handling of the
   intentional differences (notably the runtime copy currently has no
   Quick-lookup index by design — it's a corpus-loader-ingested doc,
   not a human-navigated one).

**Recommendation**: option 1 first (one-commit reach), with option 3
deferred to a later "advisor corpus pipeline" DEC if drift recurs after
option 1 is in place.

## How to add a new V-finding

1. Industrial case surfaces a death mode not in V1-V13
2. Add a row here with: Surface / Engineer symptom / Root cause /
   Fix / Status / Reference case / Lesson
3. If the death mode generalizes → add a new section (S11+) to
   `solver_convergence_playbook.md`
4. If the death mode requires main-project code change → file as
   sub-DEC under DEC-V61-198 (or a future M2.5/M4-class DEC)
5. If the death mode reveals a new cross-cutting pattern → add to
   "Cross-cutting patterns observed" above

### V89 · `cellZoneInside insidePoint` coordinate authored against an STL volume that does not contain that point — fluid-region analog of V85 surfaces at sub-pitch fin/channel geometry [case_011 v1 2026-05-09 · surfaced 2026-05-13 by case_011 v2 sub-session verification]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | case_011 v1 sediment 2026-05-09; root-cause exposed during case_011 v2 sub-session 2026-05-13 when the V85-fix attempt produced v2/v3 meshes with `region_hot_fluid` cellZone collapsed to 0 cells despite all other V85 fixes applied. v1 dict's `refinementSurfaces.region_hot_fluid.insidePoint (0.090 0.060 0.045)` and `refinementSurfaces.region_cold_fluid.insidePoint (0.090 0.060 0.027)` use `(x, y)` = `(0.090, 0.060)` as "envelope center" — but build_cad.py's hot pack geometry places channels at `y = 25.5 + idx*3.5 .. 25.5 + idx*3.5 + 2.5` (pitch 3.5mm, gap 2.5mm, fin 1.0mm); channel 9 ends at y=59.5mm, fin spans [59.5, 60.5]mm, channel 10 starts at y=60.5mm. **y=60mm falls in fin space (solid), NOT in hot_fluid channel space (fluid)**. Equivalent for cold along x: cold_pack pitch=3.5mm starting at x=27.5, channel 18 = [90.5, 93.0], fin [90.0, 90.5] → x=90mm is in cold-fin. **Both fluid insidePoints in v1 were in solid space**, not in their named cellZone's STL volume |
| Engineer symptom | sHM accepts the dict without FATAL or WARNING (the seed coordinate is valid syntactically and is "near" the surface). cellZone walk seeds from the named cell, which is geometrically inside region_solid.stl, not inside region_hot_fluid.stl (or region_cold_fluid.stl). At v1's level (1,2), the named cell is 1mm cubed straddling fin geometry; walk extends within solid-fin space (bounded by region_hot_fluid faceZone at fin-fluid interface), not into channels. Result: cellZone region_hot_fluid contains a few solid-fin cells (visible in v2/v3 result as 0 cells after solid walk reclaimed them) instead of the channel cells. The 6 cellZone-walk-pass FOAM warnings ("Cell N at (x y z) is inside cellZone region_solid from (0.0191) but already marked as being in zone region_hot_fluid") observed in v2 are a side-effect: solid walk arrives at fin cells already mis-tagged region_hot_fluid by the wrong seed, and reclaims them — accidentally correcting the symptom while masking the root cause |
| Root cause | Two co-located defects in case-author intake. **(a) Envelope-center heuristic**: case_011's author used `(L/2, W/2)` = `(0.090, 0.060)` as the canonical "interior point" for all three regions, varying only z. This heuristic is correct ONLY when the geometry is uniform along x or y at that height (e.g., a single fluid box). For periodic-fin geometry with pitch < envelope_width, "center of envelope" coincidentally falls in a fin gap (sub-pitch arithmetic). **(b) Missing pre-mesh validator**: no advisor checks that each `cellZoneInside insidePoint` coordinate is geometrically inside its associated STL's enclosed volume. The check is cheap (CGAL ray-cast or simple bbox-and-then-tetrahedral-test) but not declared anywhere in the build_cad → dict pipeline. **V89 family**: same dict-orchestration error class as V85 (solid insidePoint in fluid layer) but for fluid regions and at sub-pitch scale instead of stack-margin scale. V85 caught the most visible defect (because solid cellZone is "expected to be small" so its disappearance was less alarming) but missed the symmetric defects on fluid sides |
| Fix | Per-case sub-session action (this session, applied in v4): **(1)** Recompute fluid insidePoints from build_cad's `hot_pack_geometry()` / `cold_pack_geometry()` channel centers: hot ch.9 mid → `(0.090, 0.05825, 0.045)`; cold ch.18 mid → `(0.09175, 0.060, 0.027)`. **(2)** Move top-level `locationInMesh` to a DISTINCT channel center to avoid collision with the hot cellZone seed (use ch.10 mid `(0.090, 0.06175, 0.045)` — same z as hot but different y, so two different walks in same connected component but with distinct seed cells). **Cross-case methodology fix (Pillar-2 candidate)**: `insidePoint_validator` advisor — for each cellZone with `cellZoneInside insidePoint`, validate the named coordinate falls inside the associated STL's enclosed volume (use trimesh.ray.ray_triangle.contains_points or equivalent). Run pre-mesh at config-emit time. Promotion gate requires 2nd case with sub-pitch fin geometry (case_002b is candidate — single-stream CHT with shell-plate solids). Counts under M-A8 (`shm_dict_validator` umbrella) or its own M-A9 dispatch |
| Status | open · per-case fix landed in case_011 v4 2026-05-13 (sub-DEC `2026-05-13_v61_198_sub_case_011_v2_fix_verification.md`) · cross-case validator extraction deferred to N≥2 sub-pitch case |
| Reference case | case_011_plate_fin_compact_hx v1 baseline 2026-05-09 (`case/system/snappyHexMeshDict` v1 lines 36-44 `insidePoint (0.090 0.060 0.045)` for hot and `insidePoint (0.090 0.060 0.027)` for cold; `scripts/build_cad.py` `hot_pack_geometry()` lines 137-145 + `cold_pack_geometry()` lines 147-156 for channel positions). v2 sub-session evidence: `~/Desktop/case_011_plate_fin_compact_hx/evidence/v2/REPORT.md` §1 (attempts table, v1→v2→v3→v4 progression) + §4 (V89 candidate root-cause analysis). Retro: `.planning/retrospectives/2026-05-13_track_c_advisor_e2e_session_2_case_011.md` §9 (v2 fix path spec) |
| Lesson | The case-author's mental model of "envelope center" works for box geometry but fails for periodic-fin geometry at sub-pitch scale. **Methodology pattern**: when CAD generation uses ANY periodic-structure parameter (pitch, gap, layer count), the dict author must NOT use "envelope center" or similar uniform-geometry heuristic — every coordinate must be derived from the periodic structure's lattice (e.g., "channel N mid" rather than "envelope mid"). **Connecting V89 to V85**: same defect family, different scale. V85 caught solid-region insidePoint at sub-stack-margin scale (5.9mm offset from authored origin); V89 catches fluid-region insidePoint at sub-pitch scale (1.0mm fin offset from envelope center). Both should be closed by the SAME cross-case validator (`insidePoint_validator` advisor). **Cross-application to V90**: V89's fix in v4 sub-session would have worked at v2 if I had also fixed it then; the v3 attempt with `locationsInMesh` modern syntax (V90) was triggered because v2 still failed despite V85's insidePoint fix — the V89 defect was uncovered only by exhaustive process of elimination across 3 syntactic attempts. Captures "load-bearing root cause" can hide BEHIND a more-visible co-located defect (V85 distracted from V89 for the v1 sediment) |

### V90 · Modern `locationsInMesh ((point) zoneName)` list syntax not compatible with separate-STL multi-region case (3+ watertight STLs each defining one region) — sHM walks cellZones BEFORE surface cuts and produces empty named cellZones [case_011 v3 attempt 2026-05-13]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | case_011 v2 sub-session 2026-05-13 attempt v3: switched from old syntax (`cellZoneInside insidePoint` per refinementSurface + top-level `locationInMesh`) to modern OpenFOAM 2312 idiom (`locationsInMesh ( ((x y z) zoneName) ... )` list at top of castellatedMeshControls, with each refinementSurface declaring just `cellZone <name>` + `cellZoneInside inside`). Motivation: v2 with old syntax had top-level `locationInMesh` coinciding with hot's `insidePoint` at `(0.090 0.060 0.045)`, creating a "none" cellZone that collided with region_hot_fluid in 6 cellZone-walk passes. Modern syntax names every seed explicitly with its target zone — no implicit "none" zone, no collision possible by construction. Plausible-looking fix that produces a WORSE outcome |
| Engineer symptom | v3 sHM completes without FATAL (5140s, 15.20M cells, exit 0 — same scale as v2/v4). cellZone walk diagnostic in v3 sHM log: `For cellZone region_hot_fluid found point (0.09 0.06 0.045) in global region 0 out of 1 regions` (vs v2's `region 2 out of 271 regions` and v4's `region 1 of 271 regions`). The "out of 1 regions" tells us the cellZone walk happens BEFORE surface cuts have created the 271-component topology. After surface cuts and snap, `splitMeshRegions -cellZones -overwrite` reports **152 mesh regions** (vs v4's 6) and only **`region_cold_fluid`** is mapped to a region; hot and solid cellZones have 0 cells in the final cellZones file |
| Root cause | OpenFOAM 2312 sHM with `locationsInMesh` list syntax is designed for the SINGLE-STL multi-region case (one watertight STL defining multiple sub-regions via `regions { ... }` sub-dict in refinementSurfaces) — the canonical example is `tutorials/heatTransfer/chtMultiRegionHeater` (heater + air STLs combined). With ONE STL covering everything, sHM can walk cellZones at the un-cut mesh level because each named location is unambiguously inside its sub-region's portion of the STL. For SEPARATE-STL cases (case_011 has 3 STLs: hot, cold, solid), each STL is its own watertight boundary; sHM needs the surface cuts to applied BEFORE it can determine cellZone connectivity (the cellZones are the connected components of cells between surfaces). The old syntax does this naturally (per-surface cellZoneInside is processed after the corresponding surface is cut); the new syntax violates this ordering and the cellZones end up empty or fragmentary. Both syntaxes are valid; they target different geometry topologies |
| Fix | Per-case sub-session action: revert to old syntax for separate-STL cases (this session, applied in v4). **Cross-case methodology fix (Pillar-2 candidate)**: `shm_dict_validator` (M-A8) should detect the topology mismatch — if `refinementSurfaces` lists ≥2 separate STL names AND `locationsInMesh` is used, warn the user to use old syntax with per-surface `cellZoneInside insidePoint`. The check: count distinct STL filenames referenced; if >1 and using `locationsInMesh` list, the dict idiom is mismatched. Reciprocal check for single-STL with `cellZoneInside insidePoint` is also worth adding but less critical. **Authoritative reference**: OpenFOAM 2312 sHM source `meshRefinement::baffleAndSplitMesh()` for the BEFORE/AFTER-cut ordering proof |
| Status | open · per-case fix landed in case_011 v4 2026-05-13 (revert to old syntax) · cross-case `shm_dict_validator` topology-mismatch check deferred to M-A8 advisor implementation (currently scoped against V86 features-wiring check; V90 check is a natural extension) |
| Reference case | case_011_plate_fin_compact_hx v3 attempt 2026-05-13 (`case/system/snappyHexMeshDict` reverted to v4 in this session; v3 dict diff visible in REPORT.md §1 attempts table + git history). v3 sHM log analyzed in `evidence/v2/REPORT.md` §1 attempts row 3 + §4 V90 candidate. Retro: `.planning/retrospectives/2026-05-13_track_c_advisor_e2e_session_2_case_011.md` |
| Lesson | OpenFOAM idiom migration is NOT a syntactic rewrite — different idioms target different geometry topologies and produce different cellZone-walk orderings. **Methodology pattern**: when a tutorial example uses syntax X for geometry Y, do NOT assume syntax X is the universal "modern" replacement for legacy syntax Z — verify that X targets the SAME geometry topology as your case. **Connecting V90 to existing patterns**: same family as V47 (minMedialAxisAngle typo — OF 2312 raised FATAL but older OF silently rejected), V58 (build_domain_patches construction silently overlapping), V81 (Codex CAD emitted inlet/outlet as solids — silent sealed-room), V86 (orphaned .eMesh outputs — silent). The thread is **"OpenFOAM accepts the input as syntactically valid AND produces a mesh that looks valid; the failure surfaces only in a downstream metric that the author wasn't watching"** (here: `splitMeshRegions -cellZones` reports 152 regions instead of expected 5-6, and only 1 of 3 named cellZones populated). Pattern 4 closure: dict-validator at config-time, before sHM runs |

### V91 · V41 sediment-state correction — janafThermo Tlow=200K patch is INCOMPLETE in case_009 v1 production state: 13/53 species (incl. N2, AR, CH3O — all in active 21-species set) still have Tlow=300K; mech-loader script does NOT automate Tlow rewrite; 14.7M warning lines persist in production logs [case_009 v1 retroactive verification 2026-05-13 by Track C session 4]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | `~/Desktop/case_009_sandia_flame_d/case/constant/thermo.compressibleGas` (converted thermo dict) + `scripts/08b_load_chemistry_mech.py` (mech-loader) + `case/log_cold.txt` + `case/log_ignite.txt` (production logs) |
| Engineer symptom | V41 entry asserts `[VALIDATED 2026-05-08]` with "Verified by inspection of converted constant/thermo.compressibleGas: all species now show `Tlow 200; Thigh 3500;`" and "After the patch, cold-flow runs cleanly with no warning flood". Track C session 4 blind-verifies and **contradicts both claims**: (a) `grep "Tlow[[:space:]]\+300;" thermo.compressibleGas` returns 13 species — `N2`, `HOCN`, `C3H8`, `H2CN`, `HCCOH`, `CH2CHO`, `HNCO`, `HCCO`, `C3H7`, `CH3O`, `HCNN`, `HCNO`, `AR`; (b) of those, **N2, AR, CH3O are all present in the active 21-species set** of DRM-19 (with N2 + AR being the dominant species of `coflow_air` at Y_N2=0.768 + Y_AR=0.012); (c) `grep -c "out of temperature range" case/log_cold.txt` = **5,828,511** warnings; `case/log_ignite.txt` = **8,860,176** warnings → **14.69M warning lines combined**, log files **~58 MB combined**. The warning flood V41 claimed was eliminated is in fact still present in v1 production logs at multi-million-line scale |
| Root cause | The V41 patch recipe ("Edit thermo30.dat header before chemkinToFoam: `sed 's/^   300.000  1000.000  5000.000/   200.000  1000.000  5000.000/'`. Idempotent; preserves per-species records") was documented as bundled into `scripts/08b_load_chemistry_mech.py:patch_thermo_header` (per V41 Reference-case line: "this function name covers both V38 (THERMO ALL) and V41 (Tlow header)"). Direct file inspection of 08b lines 38-46 shows `patch_thermo_header()` only does `THERMO`→`THERMO ALL` rewrite — **the Tlow header rewrite is NOT implemented**. The Tlow=200 patch was applied **manually** (outside the script) at v1 sediment time and got applied **partially** — most species inherited Tlow=200 from the manually-rewritten global header, but 13 species still carry per-species Tlow=300 records that override the header (per janafThermo precedence: per-species Tlow/Thigh takes priority over global header). Three independent defects compound: **(a)** mech-loader doesn't automate V41 patch; **(b)** the v1 manual patch missed per-species records (only the global header was edited); **(c)** V41 sediment was authored aspirationally ("all species now show Tlow 200" verified only on the global header, not on per-species records). Combined with the **0/T fuel_jet boundary condition fixedValue=294K** (below N2/AR Tlow=300), warnings fire at every chemistry source-term evaluation for every cell in the fuel-jet region. **Sediment-state defect**: V41 is currently marked `[VALIDATED]` but should be `[QUESTIONABLE]` pending patch completion |
| Fix | Two-track. **(a) V41 sediment correction (this row)**: amend V41 status `[VALIDATED 2026-05-08]` → `[QUESTIONABLE 2026-05-13 — patch incomplete per V91]`; cross-link V91 → V41 in both corpora. **(b) Patch completion (case_009 v1.5 / v2 sub-session, OUT-OF-SCOPE for Track C session 4)**: extend `08b_load_chemistry_mech.py:patch_thermo_header` to additionally walk per-species records and rewrite `Tlow=300` → `Tlow=200` where the per-species lowCpCoeffs polynomial supports it (most NASA-9 records are fitted from 200K). Approximate 25-LOC extension. Alternative quick-fix: **bump 0/T `fuel_jet` boundary from 294K to 300K** matching the coflow_air bump documented in `0/T` inline comment (REPORT.md §Limitations row 3 acknowledges only coflow bumped, not fuel_jet — leaving fuel_jet at 294K is the other half of the same band-aid). **Cross-case methodology fix (Pillar-2 candidate)**: **A10 candidate registered** — `thermo_polynomial_range_advisor.py`: for each species in active mechanism set + each boundary `fixedValue T` in 0/T, assert `Tlow + safety_margin ≤ T_BC ≤ Thigh - safety_margin`. Promotion gate requires 2nd reacting-low-Mach case (case_009 currently only such case in 16-case roster — defer extraction until M-NUMERICS adds another reacting case or until a thermo-range issue surfaces in a non-reacting case via similar mechanism) |
| Status | open · sediment correction landed this row · V41 status amendment pending separate edit · patch completion deferred to case_009 v1.5 sub-session · A10 advisor deferred to 2nd-reacting-case extraction gate |
| Reference case | case_009_sandia_flame_d v1 baseline 2026-05-08, retroactive blind verification 2026-05-13 by Track C session 4. Direct evidence: `~/Desktop/case_009_sandia_flame_d/case/constant/thermo.compressibleGas` lines 79-101 (N2 Tlow=300), lines 245-267 region (AR Tlow=300) + 11 other species; `scripts/08b_load_chemistry_mech.py:patch_thermo_header` (lines 38-46, only THERMO ALL); `case/log_cold.txt` (5.8M janaf warnings) + `case/log_ignite.txt` (8.8M janaf warnings); `case/0/T` boundary block (fuel_jet fixedValue 294, coflow_air bumped to 300 per inline comment); v1 `REPORT.md` §Limitations row 3 ("Coflow T = 300 K vs Sandia spec 291 K — 3% perturbation, documented") acknowledges only coflow bump, not fuel_jet leftover. Retro: `.planning/retrospectives/2026-05-13_track_c_advisor_e2e_session_4_case_009.md` |
| Lesson | **Sediment-state truth depends on what was verified vs what was claimed**. V41 was authored at the moment chemkinToFoam succeeded + cold-flow ran without crashing — but "ran without crashing" was conflated with "ran cleanly". A 5.8M-warning cold-flow run is technically not a crash; the warnings are non-fatal log noise. The V41 sediment status `[VALIDATED]` codified the **non-crash** outcome and projected forward the **patch-complete** claim, both treated as if they were the same verification. They were not. **Methodology pattern (this V91 row's main lesson)**: V-series sediment-status verifications must explicitly distinguish (1) "the underlying defect is gone" from (2) "the user-facing symptom is no longer fatal". Treat them as orthogonal axes — sediment should record both. **For V41 specifically**: per-species record verification (`grep -c "Tlow[[:space:]]\+300" thermo.compressibleGas`) is the cheap defining check; warning-count verification (`grep -c "out of temperature range" log_*.txt`) is the cheap user-facing check. Both should have been required at sediment time. **Cross-application to other V-rows**: V38 (THERMO ALL) and V39 (END terminator) are similarly worth re-verifying — they ARE in the 08b script, so they should still be patch-complete, but the same skepticism applies. **Track C session 4's primary methodological contribution**: a blind reader of v1 production logs can falsify the parent sediment row in 3 grep commands. The mech-loader gap (08b doesn't automate V41) is the second-order finding — fixing the loader is easy; the harder cross-case work is **building sediment-status verification into the V-series workflow** (any new row claiming `[VALIDATED]` must include both the defect-level and symptom-level grep commands as artifacts). **Connecting V91 to broader patterns**: same family as V83 (mesh_ok permissive verdict semantic), V86 (orphaned .eMesh silent), V90 (sHM accepts dict but produces broken cellZones) — all are **"the artifact passes a surface check but fails the intent check"** patterns. V91 extends this to V-series sediment itself: **the sediment artifact records the surface check but should also have recorded the intent check** |

### V92 · `cellZoneInside inside` ray-cast classification is reliable for simple-topology STLs (single watertight outer shell) but produces empty cellZone for complex-internal-void STLs (fuse_many union with internal channel-surface shells) — cold band of channels worked (3% → 115%) while solid plate+fin union failed (37% → 0%) under identical syntax [case_011 v5 attempt 2026-05-13 · per-case fix landed in v5b]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | case_011 v5 attempt 2026-05-13 (case_011 v3 sub-session). Following v4's confirmation that `cellZoneInside insidePoint` walks only ONE connected component of the post-snap mesh (cold cellZone got 1/271 component = 74k cells / 3% retention; solid got 1/271 = 5.86M cells / 37% retention; the other 268+ fragments fell to "none"→domain0..N), v5 attempted `cellZoneInside inside` for both cold and solid hypothesizing the per-cell STL-enclosure ray-cast test would be topology-agnostic. v5 measured outcome split the hypothesis: **cold succeeded dramatically (3% → 114.5% retention, 74k → 2,974,569 cells), solid REGRESSED to 0 cells / region_solid MISSING from final polyMesh** even though identical dict syntax was applied. The 5,851,024 solid-material cells were present in the mesh but routed to `domain2` (the largest non-named connected component, matching v4's solid cell count almost exactly) instead of region_solid cellZone. v5b reverts solid to insidePoint walk (37% retention, V85-family connected-component limitation but populated) while keeping cold at `cellZoneInside inside` (115% retention) — hybrid approach gives all 3 regions present + cold dramatically improved over v4 |
| Engineer symptom | sHM log emits `Found 2 closed, named surfaces. Assigning cells in/outside these surfaces to the corresponding cellZone.` — both cold and solid processed in the same pass. No FATAL or WARNING. After cellZone walk + snap, splitMeshRegions produces **99 mesh regions**: 2 named (hot 3.34M, cold 2.97M) + 97 anonymous domain* fragments. region_solid is NOT WRITTEN by splitMeshRegions because its cellZone has 0 cells. `mesh_summary.json` v3 verdict: `FAIL · only 2/3 expected regions present; missing: ['region_solid']`. chtMultiRegionSimpleFoam cannot run on the v5 mesh because `regionProperties` lists region_solid as a required solid region. Silent dict-orchestration failure: dict syntactically valid + sHM accepts both surfaces + log says both processed + half the result silently wrong |
| Root cause | `cellZoneInside inside` uses per-cell ray-cast against the STL surface: ray from cell centroid to infinity, count intersections with STL triangles, odd = inside. For a **simple-topology STL** — one connected outer shell with no internal void surfaces, exemplified by `region_cold_fluid.stl` (a single watertight band-of-channels prism, ~15k triangles) — the ray-cast unambiguously classifies cells as inside/outside; works correctly (v5 cold 115% retention). For a **complex-internal-void STL** — exemplified by `region_solid.stl` (the cadquery `fuse_many` boolean union of 5 plates + 2×21 hot fins + 2×37 cold fins, ~2k triangles representing a solid with the channels as INTERNAL VOIDS, so the STL serializes ALL surfaces including channel walls) — the ray-cast becomes ambiguous: a ray from inside a plate body crosses (a) adjacent internal void shells (each channel adds 2 crossings as the ray exits one channel-void and enters the next), (b) the outer envelope shell (1 crossing). The cumulative parity depends on the local geometry; empirically (case_011 v5) **all** solid-material cells produced even-parity crossings → classified as outside → routed to "none" cellZone → became domain2 after split. The CAD idiom (`fuse_many` of small boxes that collectively form a void-bearing topology) is a standard cadquery pattern for building plate+fin solids, but the resulting STL exposes a hostile topology to OpenFOAM's per-cell ray-cast inside-test |
| Fix | (1) **Per-case immediate** (case_011 v5b · this session): revert `region_solid.cellZoneInside` from `inside` to `insidePoint` walk + retain V85 fix's `insidePoint (0.090 0.060 0.0191)` (separator_plate_1_2 centroid) + level (3,4). Keep cold at `cellZoneInside inside` (the v5 validated configuration). v5b measured: hot 142% / cold 115.2% / solid 36.9% retention, all 3 regions present, chtMultiRegionSimpleFoam runs cleanly. The hybrid strategy is empirically optimal given the STL topology constraints. (2) **Cross-case methodology fix (Pillar-2 candidate)**: `cellZoneInside_validator` advisor that inspects each refinementSurface's STL topology and recommends syntax mode. Algorithm: (a) parse STL; (b) count distinct connected components via trimesh.connected_components or equivalent — if >1 connected shell (i.e., internal voids present), warn user that `cellZoneInside inside` may produce empty cellZone and recommend `insidePoint` walk; (c) ray-cast self-test from N random interior centroids, verify parity consistency — if >5% of probes return inconsistent parity, surface is hostile to `inside` test. Lands as sub-DEC under M-A8 (`shm_dict_validator` umbrella; V86 features-wiring + V90 topology-mismatch + V92 cellZoneInside-topology are all dict-orchestration validators). Promotion gate requires 2nd case with similar topology dichotomy (case_002b is single-stream CHT with shell solids — different topology, may confirm or refine the discriminator). (3) **CAD-side fix candidate (case_011 future / harvest-003)**: re-emit region_solid as `envelope_box.cut(hot_channel_volumes).cut(cold_channel_volumes)` rather than `fuse_many(plates + fins)`. The resulting STL would have ONE outer envelope shell + channel-shaped voids (still multiple internal surfaces but topologically simpler). Whether this fully closes the inside-test gap or only partially closes it is empirically unknown — requires test. Note: cadquery doc's "negative space" pattern is the natural idiom for this; `fuse_many` was the wrong CAD-side choice for an OpenFOAM inside-test target |
| Status | open · per-case fix landed in case_011 v5b 2026-05-13 (revert solid to insidePoint walk; keep cold at `cellZoneInside inside`) · cross-case `cellZoneInside_validator` Pillar-2 advisor deferred to N≥2 case (case_002b candidate); CAD-side fix candidate registered as harvest-003 follow-up |
| Reference case | case_011_plate_fin_compact_hx v5 attempt 2026-05-13 (`case/v4_backup/snappyHexMeshDict.v5` — preserved v5 dict with `cellZoneInside inside` for both cold + solid; `case/v4_backup/mesh_summary_v5b.json` — saved v5b retention numbers; `case/system/snappyHexMeshDict` — live v5b dict with solid reverted to insidePoint); v5 mesh evidence: `evidence/v3/mesh_summary.json` (initially v5 verdict=FAIL · region_solid missing — overwritten by v5b run; v5 data preserved in this V92 row + sub-DEC); v5 sHM log: "Found 2 closed, named surfaces" line + post-split 99 regions with domain2=5,851,024 cells (matching v4 solid count nearly exactly, confirming the solid material was meshed but routed wrong). Substrate evidence: `~/Desktop/case_011_plate_fin_compact_hx/case/v4_backup/` (v4 + v5 + v5b artifacts preserved); evidence path: `evidence/v3/REPORT.md` §1-§3 |
| Lesson | OpenFOAM's `cellZoneInside` keyword has TWO modes with different topology assumptions encoded in the algorithm: `insidePoint` walks the connected-component graph from a seed (correct only when the named region is one connected post-snap component — fragile when surface refinement fractures topology, exemplified by V85's 271-fragment limitation); `inside` performs per-cell ray-cast against the STL (correct only when STL has ONE watertight outer shell with no internal void surfaces — fragile for fuse_many-derived solids exemplified by this V92). The CHOICE of mode must match the STL's topology, not the user's syntactic preference. **Methodology pattern**: this is the **third orthogonal failure mode** in the case_011 dict-orchestration family (V85 = solid insidePoint coordinate in wrong material; V89 = fluid insidePoint coordinate in fin space; V90 = modern locationsInMesh syntax for separate-STL; V92 = `cellZoneInside inside` for complex-void STL). All four share the same engineering symptom (sHM accepts dict + produces mesh + some cellZone has 0 cells silently) but address ORTHOGONAL defects. Productized dict validator must check all four. **Connecting V92 to V83 + V85 + V90**: V83 is the audit-verdict permissive-semantic pattern (mesh_ok=true with zero faces); V85 is the cellZone-walk fragmentation; V90 is the syntax-topology mismatch; V92 is the inside-test STL-topology mismatch. All four are **"the artifact passes a syntactic check but fails the intent check at a downstream metric the author wasn't watching"** patterns. **CAD-side observation**: the deeper fix for V92 is at the CAD-emission stage (build solid as `envelope.cut(channels)` instead of `fuse_many(plates+fins)`) — this pre-empts the dict-orchestration problem by emitting STLs in a topology that's friendly to ray-cast inside-tests. **Cross-application to V89 + V91**: the connected-component-fragmentation pattern (V85's `insidePoint` only captures 1/N components) is a **structural** failure of the cellZone walk algorithm under thin-feature surface refinement; the inside-test STL-topology fragility (V92) is a **structural** failure of the ray-cast algorithm under fuse_many CAD output. Both are upstream CAD/dict-stage failures masked as downstream mesh-stage failures. **Sediment author**: Claude Code Opus 4.7 (1M context) · Track C session 2 case_011 v3 sub-session · 2026-05-14 |

### V93 · Reacting low-Mach pre-ignition T floor rule — `min(boundary fixedValue T)` must satisfy `≥ max(per-species Tlow in constant/thermo.compressibleGas)`; chemkinToFoam can leave per-species Tlow=300 even after a global-header patch (V41), so post-conversion sweep is mandatory [case_009 v1.5 cleanup 2026-05-14, regression evidence on V41]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | OpenFOAM `janafThermo<EquationOfState>::limit()` per-species temperature-range check; downstream of `chemkinToFoam` output `constant/thermo.compressibleGas`. Affected solver class: reactingFoam + reactingPimpleFoam + chtMultiRegionReactingFoam (all `hePsiThermo + janafThermo + reactingMixture` stacks) |
| Engineer symptom | After applying V41 global-header rewrite (`200.000 1000.000 5000.000` substitution into therm.dat line 2), reactingFoam *still* floods the log with `janafThermo<EquationOfState>::limit: attempt to use ... out of temperature range 300 -> N; T = <inlet_T>` warnings whenever any cold inlet has T < 300 K, even with the global Tlow header now at 200. The flood originates from 13 / 53 GRI-3.0 species (N2, AR, CH3O, HCCO, HCCOH, HCNN, HCNO, HNCO, HOCN, CH2CHO, H2CN, C3H7, C3H8) whose chemkin records declare per-species validity at `G 300.000 <Thigh> 1000.000` — chemkinToFoam writes those per-species Tlow values into the converted dict regardless of what the global header says. In case_009 v1, **N2 at Tlow=300 + fuel_jet boundary T=294 K** alone produced **8.86M warnings in 593 μs of ignite time** before the solver effectively stalled |
| Root cause | chemkinToFoam transports TWO independent temperature-range channels into `constant/thermo.compressibleGas`: (a) the **global header** (therm.dat line 2) sets default Tlow/Tcommon/Thigh used by species whose per-species record is silent on T-range; (b) the **per-species record** (CHEMKIN line 1 of each 4-line block, columns 46-73) overrides the global default for that species. V41 only addressed channel (a). For combustion mechanisms shipped with mixed-vintage thermo data (GRI-3.0 is exactly this — most species at 200-3500 K but ~25% inherited from older NIST polynomials at 300-5000 K), channel (b) is the dominant defect. Compounded by `0/T fuel_jet fixedValue 294` being below the per-species Tlow=300 of N2 (the dominant fuel_jet diluent at Y=0.65), every cell in the fuel-jet column generates one warning per species per inner PIMPLE iteration → log explodes + stderr I/O dominates wall-clock + chemistry ODE substep timestepping fights the limiter. **The case is functionally unrunnable until per-species Tlow is also lowered to the cold-inlet floor** |
| Fix | **General rule** (this V93): for every reacting low-Mach simulation, before launching the solver, verify `min(boundary fixedValue T over species-active patches) - safety_margin ≥ max(per-species Tlow in constant/thermo.compressibleGas)`. When inequality fails, run a post-conversion sweep that lowers `Tlow N;` → `Tlow floor;` for each species block where `N > floor`. **Concrete script** (case_009 v1.5 deliverable): `~/Desktop/case_009_sandia_flame_d/v1_5/scripts/patch_janaf_tlow.py` — argparse CLI, idempotent, writes `.pre_tlow_patch` backup, prints (species, old, new) audit table; uses a brace-depth-aware parser so it edits only top-level species blocks. Safety: 100 K extrapolation 300→200 K of the published lowCpCoeffs polynomial is exact for monatomic AR (cp = 5R/2), <1% cp(T) error for diatomic N2 (verified against NIST-JANAF tables), and physically irrelevant for trace species (Y_k ~ 10^-30 at cold inlets makes any cp_k error vanish in `Y_k * cp_k`). **Recommended bundling**: extend `08b_load_chemistry_mech.py:patch_thermo_header` (renamed `patch_thermo_metadata`) with `patch_janaf_tlow.py` semantics so future reacting cases inherit the fix automatically. **Cross-case advisor candidate (NOT promoted this session)**: A10 `thermo_polynomial_range_advisor.py` proposed in V91 — read active species set + read `0/T` boundary blocks + read converted thermo dict → emit per-species fail/warn report. Promotion gate per V91 = 2nd reacting-low-Mach case; v1.5 supplies the 2nd evidence row but Track C session 5 explicitly defers `land` of A10 to a later sub-DEC |
| Status | `[VALIDATED 2026-05-14]` — v1.5 e2e regression evidence: post-patch reactingFoam log contains **0 instances of janafThermo limit warnings** and Tmax climbs from pilot floor 1880 K through 1900-2050+ K demonstrating ignition propagation (v1 was stuck at 1880 K). v1.5 log size: `<10 MB` for 2000-timestep ignite vs v1's 3.2 GB for 593 timesteps → 7000× I/O reduction per timestep. Per-species Tlow audit: `grep -c "Tlow[[:space:]]\+300" v1_5/case/constant/thermo.compressibleGas == 0`; `grep -c "Tlow[[:space:]]\+200" == 53` |
| Reference case | case_009_sandia_flame_d v1.5 cleanup 2026-05-14 (Track C session 5, this session). Evidence: `~/Desktop/case_009_sandia_flame_d/v1_5/case/constant/thermo.compressibleGas` (53/53 species at Tlow=200); `v1_5/case/constant/thermo.compressibleGas.pre_tlow_patch` (backup proving the 13-species delta); `v1_5/scripts/patch_janaf_tlow.py` (parametric idempotent patch tool); `v1_5/case/log_ignite_v1_5.txt` (0 limit warnings) vs `case/log_ignite.txt` (8.86M limit warnings); `v1_5/evidence/{convergence,Tmax_vs_time}.png` + `species_max.json` (chemistry progression). Companion sub-DEC: `.planning/decisions/2026-05-14_v61_198_sub_case_009_v1_5_cleanup.md` |
| Lesson | The reacting-low-Mach numerics class has a **two-channel mechanism-loader vulnerability** that single-channel fixes can't close. V41 found channel (a); V93 closes channel (b). The cross-case takeaway is that **mechanism-loader extraction (DEC-V61-198 sub-DEC bundling V38+V39+V40+V41+V93) must validate against the CONVERTED dict, not the SOURCE therm.dat** — the converter has internal state we don't control, so input-stage patches alone are insufficient. **Pre-ignition T-floor formal rule**: every reacting-low-Mach case must run `min(boundary T) - 5K ≥ max(per-species Tlow)` as an explicit pre-flight check before allocating solver wall-clock; the cheapest place to land this is as a `pre_flight_checks/check_thermo_range.py` step in the run wrapper. **Methodology pattern**: same family as V83/V86/V90/V91/V92 — the "syntactic check passes, intent check fails silently" pattern — but the failure surface here is **converter-mediated** (chemkinToFoam is the silent gap), not user-syntax-mediated. This argues that future advisors should distinguish (i) syntax-stage validators (catch user errors before tool invocation) from (ii) output-stage validators (catch converter/builder errors after tool invocation); V93's `patch_janaf_tlow.py` is an output-stage validator + auto-remediator. **Sediment author**: Claude Code Opus 4.7 (1M context) · Track C session 5 case_009 v1.5 sub-session · 2026-05-14 |

### V94 · STL files emitted by cq.exporters.export() carry NO face-zone labels — single-shell watertight surfaces lose CAD-stage face names; downstream sHM creates only one undifferentiated boundary patch per region pair, so any case_profile referencing named inlet/outlet/external-wall patches cannot host the intended flow physics [case_011 v1 2026-05-09 · surfaced 2026-05-14 by case_011 v3 sub-session solver e2e attempt]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | case_011 v1 sediment 2026-05-09; root-cause exposed during case_011 v3 sub-session 2026-05-14 when chtMultiRegionSimpleFoam attempted to read `0/region_hot_fluid/T` BC for the patch `region_hot_fluid_to_domain0` and failed with `Cannot find patchField entry for region_hot_fluid_to_domain0`. Diagnostic deeper: examined per-region polyMesh/boundary files — `region_hot_fluid` has exactly 2 patches (`_to_domain0`, `_to_region_solid`), NO `hot_inlet`, NO `hot_outlet`, NO `bgFront/bgBack/bgLeft/bgRight/bgBottom/bgTop`. Same for cold (74 patches: 1 `_to_region_solid` + 73 `_to_domain*`, NO `cold_inlet/cold_outlet/bg*`). The BC files in `0.orig/region_hot_fluid/{T,p,p_rgh,U}` reference `hot_inlet`, `hot_outlet`, and `(bgFront\|...\|bgTop)` patches that do NOT exist in the actual mesh — these BC entries are dangling (OF gracefully ignores them), and the patches that DO exist (`_to_domain*`) lacked BC entries until the v3 sub-session added `"region_X_to_domain.*" { type zeroGradient; }` catch-all entries to T fields |
| Engineer symptom | (a) Pre-solver: case appears properly configured — 4-field BCs per fluid region with named inlet/outlet/bg patches, 1-field BC for solid with coupled-baffle interfaces. (b) Solver invocation: FATAL `Cannot find patchField entry for region_hot_fluid_to_domain0` — the BC file references inlets/outlets/bg that don't exist AND lacks entry for the interfaces that do. (c) After BC catch-all fix + adding `0/region_solid/p` + adding SIMPLE block to solid fvSolution, solver runs cleanly. (d) Solver iter 1+: hot+cold U boundary at all walls = fixedValue (0,0,0) → no momentum source → NO MASS FLOW. (e) Solver iter 200: hot region T equilibrated 420 K → 360 K, cold 300 K → 360 K, solid 360 K → 360 K — all three regions diffuse to the conduction-equilibrium midpoint (~360 K), not the Kays-London predicted T_h_out=364 K + T_c_out=350 K outlet-flow temperatures. (f) The solver works; the case physics is degenerate — case_011 cannot host the convective recuperator regime its case profile declares |
| Root cause | Two compounding defects in the CAD-stage → mesh-stage data flow. **(a) cq.exporters.export(wp_m, str(out), exportType="STL", tolerance=2e-4, ...)** (scripts/01_extract_surfaces.py line 36-42) emits each region as a SINGLE WATERTIGHT STL surface without face-zone metadata. cadquery's STL exporter tessellates the solid into triangles but discards CAD face boundaries — there is no way to label "this triangle is the inlet face" vs "this triangle is the channel wall". As a result, sHM sees the entire region surface as one undifferentiated `triSurfaceMesh` named `region_hot_fluid` (et al.), and creates only one boundary patch per region pair (or one per fragmented connected component). **(b) BC files in 0.orig/region_*/** were authored aspirationally — the case author wrote BCs assuming named inlet/outlet patches would exist in the mesh (per the case profile's ε-NTU 0.466 / Q≈225 W flow-through HX target with hot 0.004 kg/s + cold 0.0045 kg/s mass-flow inlets), but the STL emission path does not preserve face labels, so no such patches ever materialize. **Pillar-2 cross-application of V83 + V92 + V81**: V83 said "audit verdict OK but downstream test missing" (mesh_ok=true with zero faces); V92 said "dict syntax accepted but cellZone empty" (cellZoneInside inside silent failure); V81 said "AI-emitted CAD's inlet/outlet bodies treated as walls by sHM" (cylinders without face labels → no inlet patches); V94 is the **same family for cadquery-emitted STLs at scale** — the engineer assumes face labels survive the cq.exporters→STL→sHM pipeline, but they don't. The case_011 case_profile + 0.orig BCs documented a flow-through HX; the substrate built a sealed conduction box |
| Fix | (1) **Per-case immediate** (case_011 v3 sub-session, this session): add catch-all wildcard BCs `"region_X_to_domain.*" { type zeroGradient; }` to T files (cold's many `_to_domain*` interfaces); add `0/region_solid/p` file (chtMRSimpleFoam OF v2312 requires p in solid region even for incompressible heSolidThermo, per cpuCabinet tutorial); add SIMPLE block to solid fvSolution. Accept that solver runs degenerate (no inlet/outlet → no flow → pure conduction). Documented as caveat in v3 REPORT.md §5.1. (2) **Per-case CAD-side fix (case_011 future)**: refactor scripts/build_cad.py to emit hot_fluid + cold_fluid as `cq.Assembly` with `cq.Sketch.face("Tagged")` or equivalent face-naming, then 01_extract_surfaces.py writes per-face STLs (`hot_inlet.stl`, `hot_outlet.stl`, `hot_walls.stl`, etc.) plus snappyHexMeshDict's geometry block declares all of them as triSurfaceMesh with named patches. Alternatively: write a `topoSet`+`createPatch` post-mesh stage that splits the `region_hot_fluid_to_domain0` patch into `hot_inlet` (by spatial filter: faces at x=0 or x=L) + `hot_outlet` (other side) + `hot_external_walls`. Estimated 60-100 LOC. (3) **Cross-case methodology fix (Pillar-2 candidate)**: `stl_face_label_validator` advisor that inspects each `refinementSurfaces.<name>.stl` and checks whether the case_profile declares inlet/outlet patches that the STL does not support. Algorithm: parse case_profile.md for "inlet"/"outlet" keywords + parse 0.orig/*/U for `flowRateInletVelocity`/`pressureInletOutletVelocity` BC types → if found, require corresponding STL face-zone labels to be discoverable via cq.Assembly metadata OR via per-face .stl emission. Warn at config-emit time if mismatch. Lands as sub-DEC under M-A8 dict-validator umbrella (V86/V90/V92/V94 all dict-orchestration siblings) |
| Status | open · per-case fix landed in case_011 v3 sub-session 2026-05-14 (BC catch-all + solid p + solid SIMPLE block; solver runs but in degenerate pure-conduction mode) · CAD-side fix candidate registered for case_011 v6/v7 or harvest-003 promotion · cross-case `stl_face_label_validator` Pillar-2 advisor deferred to N≥2 case (any other cq.exporters-STL-based case is candidate; case_012/013/etc. that ALSO emit per-region STLs via cadquery would surface this if they also reference inlet/outlet BC patches) |
| Reference case | case_011_plate_fin_compact_hx v1 baseline 2026-05-09 (`scripts/01_extract_surfaces.py` lines 36-42 cq.exporters.export emitting single-STL-per-region; `scripts/build_cad.py:build_hot_region/build_cold_region/build_solid_region` lines 200-400 — none use cq.Assembly with face naming; `case/0.orig/region_hot_fluid/{T,p,p_rgh,U}` BC files referencing `hot_inlet/hot_outlet/bg*` non-existent patches; `case/constant/region_hot_fluid/polyMesh/boundary` showing only 2 patches `_to_domain0` + `_to_region_solid`; `case/log/05_chtMultiRegionSimpleFoam.log` first FATAL output `Cannot find patchField entry for region_hot_fluid_to_domain0`). Sub-DEC: `2026-05-14_v61_198_sub_case_011_v3_solver_e2e.md`. Substrate evidence: `~/Desktop/case_011_plate_fin_compact_hx/evidence/v3/REPORT.md` §5.1 (degenerate physics caveat) |
| Lesson | **The case-profile-claim vs substrate-capability mismatch is detectable at sediment time**. The case_011 v1 sediment documented the case as `chtMultiRegionFoam (ESI 2312, steady) ... 2 fluid streams (hot 420K, cold 300K) cross-flow ... K_manifold=2.5 ref` — flow physics. The substrate built a sealed box. The discrepancy is detectable in 3 grep commands: (a) `grep -l "inlet\|outlet" case_profiles/case_011*.md` returns matches; (b) `grep "patch.*hot_inlet" case/constant/region_hot_fluid/polyMesh/boundary` returns nothing; (c) inconsistency between (a) and (b) = sediment defect. **Methodology pattern**: V81 (Codex CAD emitted inlet/outlet as solids → walls; surfaced after solver attempts) and V94 (cadquery CAD lost face labels → no patches; surfaced after solver attempts) are the same family — **CAD-stage assumption that face labels are preserved through CAD→STL→sHM is wrong unless explicitly handled**. The two known idioms that DO preserve face labels are: (i) STEP export with name preservation + STEP→STL via FreeCAD MeshPart (which can carry STEP face labels via sidecar metadata), OR (ii) per-face STL emission with explicit naming in the snappyHexMeshDict geometry block. **Cross-application to broader CFD harness work**: any case in the 16-case roster that emits STLs from cadquery and references named inlet/outlet patches needs this audit. case_011, case_013, case_015 (CHT-LES) at minimum. **Connecting V94 to V83 + V85 + V92**: V83 = audit verdict permissive (mesh_ok with zero faces); V85 = cellZone walk fragmentation; V92 = inside-test STL topology fragility; V94 = STL face-label loss through CAD→STL pipeline. All four are CAD/dict-stage failures that show up as silent downstream defects. **Solver-runs-but-physics-degenerate is a more dangerous failure mode than solver-FATAL** because the engineer must catch it via cross-reference, not via grep-for-FATAL. **Sediment author**: Claude Code Opus 4.7 (1M context) · Track C session 2 case_011 v3 sub-session · 2026-05-14 |

### V95 · STL surface surgery to restore a phantom outlet patch FAILS when the closed-bubble feature scale is below the sHM cell size — watertightness restoration alone is not sufficient if interior cell-count < ~1 at chosen refinement level [case_002a M-APU-RESTORE Tier 1 NEGATIVE 2026-05-14]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | case_002a M-APU-RESTORE Tier 1 optional sub-session 2026-05-14: V84 F4b had identified missing `apu_intake` outlet patch as root cause (a) of p_rgh init-residual plateau 0.39–0.54 at steps 500–2689. Brief proposed STL surface surgery on body_1 louver "per V75" to restore the patch. Pre-surgery topology audit revealed two facts inverting the brief: (1) **`body_1` does not spatially overlap apu_intake** — body_1 bbox x[64890–65206]/y[1672–2190]/z[−918,−343] mm vs apu_intake bbox x[65221–65571]/y[677–933]/z[−416,99] mm, centroid distance 1.26 m; the "louver overlap" geometric premise was incorrect. (2) The v32 combined STL `apu_intake` region is 508 tri **non-watertight** — V77 F4a-strict `meshing_isotropic_explicit_remeshing` with `collapseflag=True` had broken closure of the original 68-tri watertight body (89.3 cm³ closed envelope) as a side effect of remeshing it as a "simple shell". Hypothesis: sHM cannot bisect fluid domain with non-watertight surface → no patch created. Fix = restore watertight 68-tri version (mm→m × 0.001 scale, bbox-exact match) into the combined STL via region replacement. Re-built v33 mesh end-to-end via Docker `opencfd/openfoam-default:2312` (surfaceFeatureExtract + blockMesh + snappyHexMesh + checkMesh, ~30 min Apple Silicon) |
| Engineer symptom | After watertightness restoration: sHM completes without FATAL but `constant/polyMesh/boundary` still shows **27 patches, apu_intake absent** — phantom-patch status unchanged from v32. Worse: mesh quality regressed across every metric — cells 3.10M → 3.98M (+28%), max_skewness 6.875 → **7.966** (+16%), highly skew faces 20 → **69** (+245%), concave cells 95 → **147,036** (1,547×!), under-determined cells 46 → 118 (+156%), plus 9 duplicate faces / 4 short edges / 477 warped faces appearing where there were none. The watertight restoration didn't even produce a checkMesh-FATAL state, just degraded quality across all dimensions. Engineer following V75 playbook (closed body + correct watertight topology → patch should appear) finds the patch still absent and the mesh significantly worse |
| Root cause | sHM `refinementSurfaces apu_intake { level (3 4); }` produces cells of size ~5–10 mm (background block size 80 mm ÷ 2^4 ÷ 2^3). The apu_intake closed-bubble interior is 89 cm³ packed into a 350×256×515 mm bbox — **bbox fill ratio 0.2%**, geometrically a thin envelope (effective "thickness" along its narrowest axis sub-millimeter). At cell size 5–10 mm, the bubble interior contains **~1–8 candidate cells**, far below sHM's effective threshold to seed a coherent sub-region during castellation. The bubble interior cells get merged into ambient `body_1` / `Outer_Surf` cell pool before snap stage runs, so the patch boundary at the bubble surface never materializes. Watertightness restoration is necessary but not sufficient — the patch-creation algorithm needs ≥ several cells of fluid sub-region interior to anchor a coherent boundary, and the refinement level vs feature scale mismatch precludes that. **This is a smaller-scale variant of V78**: V78 was about bbox shell-punch on open shells (50–500 mm operating scale) regressing mesh quality; V95 extends the lesson to closed-bubble watertightness restoration when feature scale < 2× cell size. The mesh-quality regression (skew 20 → 69, concave 95 → 147k) shares V78's mechanism — newly-introduced cell-scale geometry mismatch creates more sHM snap stress than it relieves |
| Fix | (1) **Per-case future-session options** (none pursued this session — brief authorized stopping at negative finding): (a) **Solver-side BC fix via `createPatch`**: extract sub-region of `farfield_cylinder` localized to apu_intake bbox, rename to `apu_intake_outlet`, apply `flowRateOutletVelocity` BC. No mesh rebuild needed. Recommended. (b) **Refinement bump** `apu_intake { level (3 4) → (5 6) }`: cells ~1.25–2.5 mm; risk ~3–4× total cell count growth (~12M, exceeds current `maxGlobalCells 11M`); likely new mesh-quality hot-spots per V76. (c) **CAD redesign**: replace apu_intake STL with thick (≥30 mm) closed envelope. Out of scope (CATIA-level). (d) **Accept residual + switch solver**: keep farfield-as-outlet physics, switch to `buoyantPimpleFoam` per V84 root cause (b). (2) **Cross-case methodology**: V95 is the closed-bubble-feature-scale cousin of V94 (STL face-label loss) and V81 (CAD emitted as solids). The shared anti-pattern: **post-hoc STL surgery cannot reconstruct patch identities lost upstream**. Either preserve face labels in CAD-stage (V94 fix), or accept solver-side BC fixes (V95 5a) rather than chasing mesh-level surgery. (3) **Restore protocol**: brief's hard constraint "v32 polyMesh 不破坏" satisfied by pre-surgery backup → post-FAIL restore via simple `cp -r`. Pattern: any STL surgery session on a substrate that has a known-good baseline polyMesh MUST backup polyMesh as a sibling dir BEFORE running 06_run_mesh.sh, not after, since 06 destructively overwrites in place |
| Status | closed negative · this session delivered the negative finding as the milestone closure; V75 status NOT flipped to fix-verified (stays partial); DEC-V61-198 "Steady-state under correct outlet physics" remains Not validated; M-APU-RESTORE Tier 1 milestone CLOSED NEGATIVE; per-case future-session options (a)/(b)/(c)/(d) registered but not scheduled |
| Reference case | case_002a APU bay 2026-05-14 M-APU-RESTORE Tier 1 sub-session (`~/Desktop/apu-bay-ventilation/evidence/v33/REPORT.md` for full evidence with quantitative regression tables; `case/constant/triSurface/cleaned_combined.v32_pre_apu_restore.stl` for surgery input STL; `case/constant/polyMesh.v33_apu_restore_failed/` for failed mesh evidence; sub-DEC `.planning/decisions/2026-05-14_v61_198_sub_apu_restore_outlet_patch.md`). Cross-refs: V75 (apu_intake phantom origin), V77 (F4a-strict isotropic remesh that introduced the watertightness regression as side effect), V78 (parent negative-finding pattern at larger feature scale), V84 (F4b parent — solver-side evidence of physics impact of missing patch), V94 (sibling at face-label-loss layer) |
| Lesson | **STL post-hoc surgery for patch restoration has a feature-scale floor**. The sHM patch-creation algorithm needs the bisected interior region to contain enough cells to anchor a coherent sub-region during castellation. Empirically for apu_intake (bbox 0.45m³, fill ratio 0.2%, refinement level 3-4 → 5-10mm cells), interior cell count ~1-8 is below threshold. **Diagnostic pattern**: before attempting STL surgery to restore a missing patch, compute `interior_cell_estimate = (interior_volume_m3) / (cell_size_m)^3` for the target feature at the planned refinement level. If estimate < ~30 cells, **STL surgery alone will not recover the patch** — bump refinement level or change strategy (solver-side BC patch creation via `createPatch`). **Connecting V95 to V78**: V78 was the negative-finding sibling at open-shell bbox-deletion scale; V95 is the closed-body watertightness-restoration scale. Both produce the same outcome class (mesh quality regression without achieving the stated fix). **Anti-pattern bundle confirmed**: V78 + V95 jointly establish that **STL surgery is a fragile lever for downstream patch-identity issues**; the more robust interventions are: (i) fix the upstream CAD/STL emission to preserve face labels (V94's solution class), (ii) accept the missing-patch state and apply solver-side BC fixes via `createPatch` (V95 5a), or (iii) accept that the case is in a degenerate sub-physics regime and document accordingly (V84 lesson 2 — case_002a F4b accepted farfield-as-outlet as "statistically stable not numerically steady"). **Solver-side BC fix (V95 5a) is the recommended path** because it operates at the level where the missing capability actually matters (BC = physics interface) rather than at the level where the missing capability is observed (mesh = geometry). **Brief-geometric-premise audit lesson**: this session's brief named "body_1 louver overlap" as the surgery target, but bbox analysis showed body_1 and apu_intake do not overlap on any axis (centroid distance 1.26 m). The "louver overlap" framing was a plausible-sounding folk explanation that fell apart in 30 seconds of trimesh inspection. **Sub-pattern**: when a brief names a specific geometric relationship as the cause, **measure it before acting** — bbox intersection / nearest-distance / centroid check costs nothing and prevents wasted compute. **Sediment author**: Claude Code Opus 4.7 (1M context) · M-APU-RESTORE Tier 1 sub-session · 2026-05-14 · confidence: high (negative finding grounded in quantitative regression across 7 mesh metrics; brief-premise inversion grounded in trimesh bbox measurement) |

### V96 · A6 `unit_detector.parse_step_header_unit()` default `max_bytes=65536` silently truncates STEP files whose unit declaration sits past byte 65536 — ST-Developer-style exports place GLOBAL_UNIT_ASSIGNED_CONTEXT at file end, hiding the INCH/MM declaration from the 64 KB scan window [case_003 v2 2026-05-14 by Track C session 6]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | case_003 v2 e2e attempt 2026-05-14 (Track C session 6) on HLPW6 CRM-HLS source STEP `inputs/cache/tier1_crm_hls_hlpw6_tc1.stp` (716,110 bytes). A6 `parse_step_header_unit(step_path)` invoked with default args, returned `(None, ["STEP header does not contain a recognized LENGTH unit declaration"])`. Manual grep `grep -nb "CONVERSION_BASED_UNIT.*INCH"` showed the declaration at **byte 707,430** of 716,110 — well past A6's 64 KB read window. Retry with `parse_step_header_unit(step_path, max_bytes=800000)` correctly returned `(GeometricUnit.INCH, ["STEP header declares CONVERSION_BASED_UNIT('INCH')"])`. The regex `_CONVERSION_INCH_RE = r"CONVERSION_BASED_UNIT\\s*\\(\\s*'INCH'"` is correct; only the byte-window assumption is wrong |
| Engineer symptom | Engineer ingests a new Tier-1 industrial STEP. A6 returns "declared_unit=None / decision=UNKNOWN / confidence=0". Combined with V97 (bbox channel also silent), the unit advisory provides zero signal. Engineer cannot tell if (a) STEP genuinely lacks a unit declaration, (b) the declaration is in an unrecognized form, or (c) the declaration is silently truncated. With no signal, downstream mesh/solver proceed on whatever scale the import library inferred (cadquery silently converted INCH→MM at importStep time), producing a 25.4× scale anomaly that surfaces only when domain bbox + force coefficients look "off" 50+ minutes into a meshed+solved run |
| Root cause | A6's docstring claim "STEP UNIT declarations sit in the early DATA section" is observably false for industrial STEP exporters using AP242 / STEP-conformant-late-context placement. ST-Developer (and any toolchain using its STEPlib), CATIA-V5 in AP242 mode, certain SolidWorks-2024 exports, and the HLPW6 publisher's toolchain all place `GLOBAL_UNIT_ASSIGNED_CONTEXT` near end of file as part of the closing geometric representation context block. The 64 KB default scan window optimizes for the SolidWorks-2018-pre-AP242 / CATIA-with-early-context pattern A6 was historically tuned against (case_002a APU bay's CATIA-mm STEP has unit decl at byte ~6,000 — fits comfortably under 64 KB). HLPW6 source STEP at 716 KB with INCH decl at byte 707,430 puts the declaration in the last 1.2% of file bytes. **Pillar-2 cross-application of V83**: surface check (regex execution) runs cleanly within the 64 KB window, finds nothing, returns None. Intent check ("does this STEP have ANY unit declaration discoverable in the file") is never asked. Same family as V83 (mesh_ok permissive), V88 (mrf_audit accept-without-cellZone-presence), V91 (V-series sediment-status as verifiable-artifact), V93 (boundary T ↔ thermo Tlow cross-artifact). This is the **6th cross-application of V83 across Track C sessions 1-6** |
| Fix | (1) **Per-advisor immediate** (sub-DEC `V61-198-sub-A6-unit-detector-truncation-and-range-tuning` candidate): bump `parse_step_header_unit` default `max_bytes` from 65,536 to 1,048,576 (1 MB). Covers all observed industrial STEP unit-declaration placements; trivial memory hit; preserves the chunk-not-stream guard for huge STEPs. Single-line constant change in `ui/backend/services/geometry_ingest/unit_detector.py`.
