(2) **Regression test**: add unit-test asserting `parse_step_header_unit(<HLPW6 source>) == (GeometricUnit.INCH, ...)` to lock the regression. (3) **Alternative fix candidates considered**: (a) stream-scan whole file with hard cap (e.g., < 16 MB) — more complete, higher memory hit; (b) two-pass scan (first 64 KB + last 64 KB) — cheap, catches both early-decl and late-decl forms but skips middle-decl placements (rare in practice); (c) make max_bytes a constructor parameter (caller decides) — pushes complexity to caller. **Recommended**: (1) + (2). |
| Status | `[VALIDATED 2026-05-14 (DEC-V61-198-sub-A6-unit-detector-hardening)]` · code+test merged: `parse_step_header_unit` default `max_bytes` 65,536 → 1,048,576 (1 MB) in `ui/backend/services/geometry_ingest/unit_detector.py`; regression test `test_parse_step_header_inch_past_64kb_window` in `ui/backend/tests/test_unit_detector.py` pins late-placed INCH detection at default args and confirms the old 64 KB ceiling still silently truncates the same fixture (counter-example assertion) |
| Reference case | `~/Desktop/case_003_crm_hls_boundary_layer/inputs/cache/tier1_crm_hls_hlpw6_tc1.stp` (size 716,110; INCH decl at byte 707,430; verifiable via `grep -nb "CONVERSION_BASED_UNIT.*INCH"`). Cross-case: `apu-bay-ventilation/.../CATIA-mm.step` (unit decl at byte ~6,000; A6 succeeds at default max_bytes — these are the two opposite-end cases that bracket the empirical placement distribution). Session 6 retro: `.planning/retrospectives/2026-05-14_track_c_session_6_case_003_crm_hls.md` §3 + §4 + §5. Evidence: `~/Desktop/case_003_crm_hls_boundary_layer/evidence/session6_advisor_xapp.txt` |
| Lesson | **Empirical-constant tuning in advisors should be calibrated against the actual file-end-placement distribution observed in industrial STEPs, not against assumed/typical "header" placement.** A6's `max_bytes=65536` was an optimization that silently rejected exactly the class of files (industrial Tier-1) that motivated A6's existence in the first place. **Methodology pattern**: when an advisor's diagnostic regex is correct but its scan-window is constant-tuned to a non-representative sample, the advisor will pass canary cases (case_002a APU bay) and fail real industrial cases (HLPW6 CRM-HLS) in a way that's not visible without case-side evidence. **Connecting V96 to V83 + V97**: V96 is V83 6th cross-application AT the advisor's own internal logic — a regex channel passes (no match found, returns None), the intent check (does the file actually contain a discoverable unit declaration anywhere?) is never asked. V97 is the cousin via the OTHER channel (bbox-range) — both channels' silences combine into a false negative for industrial-scale geometry. **Cross-application**: any A6-style advisor using max_bytes-truncated file scans needs an audit: STEP_canonicalizer, FreeCAD STEP loaders, anything that reads STEP headers without full-file context. **Sediment author**: Claude Code Opus 4.7 (1M context) · Track C session 6 case_003 v2 dispatched sub-session · 2026-05-14 · confidence: high (verifiable by single `parse_step_header_unit` retry with widened max_bytes) |

### V97 · A6 `unit_detector.bbox_plausible_units()` default range `(0.01 m, 100 m)` rejects valid industrial-aircraft / ship / civil-structure scale geometries — 100 m upper bound is empirically biased toward small reference geometries [case_003 v2 2026-05-14 by Track C session 6]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | case_003 v2 e2e attempt 2026-05-14 (Track C session 6) on HLPW6 CRM-HLS airframe_reference raw bbox max extent = **182,880** (loaded mm units). A6 `bbox_plausible_units(182880, range=_INDUSTRIAL_EXTENT_RANGE_M)` invoked with default `_INDUSTRIAL_EXTENT_RANGE_M = (0.01, 100.0)` returned `()` (empty tuple). Under candidate units: MM (1e-3) → 182.88 m rejected (> 100 m cap); CM (1e-2) → 1828.80 m rejected; M (1.0) → 182880 m rejected; INCH (0.0254) → 4645 m rejected. All four candidate units fall outside the (0.01, 100) m window. Retry with widened range `bbox_plausible_units(182880, (0.01, 1000.0))` correctly returned `(GeometricUnit.MM,)` |
| Engineer symptom | Engineer ingests an industrial-aircraft-scale STEP (full aircraft, ship section, wind farm domain, civil structure). A6 bbox-channel returns empty plausible set → silent. Combined with V96 (header-truncation also silent), A6.detect_unit decision=UNKNOWN, confidence=0. Engineer receives zero advisory signal. Downstream mesh proceeds on whatever scale the import library inferred. Force coefficients / Re / y+ all silently wrong-by-25.4× if the underlying issue is also unit-conversion |
| Root cause | The `_INDUSTRIAL_EXTENT_RANGE_M = (0.01, 100.0)` upper bound is observably below the operating envelope of real industrial CFD: full aircraft (CRM 58.7 m wingspan, A380 79.8 m, transport jets up to ~80 m), ships (ferries 200 m, container ships 400 m), wind farms (rotor diameters 100-250 m, multi-turbine domains 1+ km), civil structures (buildings 300+ m, bridges spanning 1+ km), atmospheric BL CFD (1+ km vertical extent), wind-tunnel test sections (10-40 m), space launch facilities (50-100+ m). 100 m cap captures roughly the academic-reference middle of this distribution (single airfoils, heat-exchangers, small assemblies, single rooms) and rejects the upper tail. **Pillar-2 cross-application of V83 + companion of V96**: bbox-channel surface check runs cleanly (computes whether raw extent falls in range under each candidate unit), finds nothing fits, returns empty tuple. Intent check ("is this geometry plausibly large-but-still-industrial — should I widen?") is never asked. Same family as V96 (truncation), V83 (mesh_ok permissive), V88 (mrf accept), V91 (V-series sediment), V93 (boundary T ↔ thermo Tlow). 6th cross-application of V83 sharing V96's underlying advisor module |
| Fix | (1) **Per-advisor immediate** (same sub-DEC as V96 — `V61-198-sub-A6-unit-detector-truncation-and-range-tuning`): widen `_INDUSTRIAL_EXTENT_RANGE_M` to `(0.01, 1000.0)`. Covers all observed full-aircraft + ship + civil-structure geometries. Single-line constant change in `unit_detector.py`. (2) **Regression test**: add unit-test asserting `bbox_plausible_units(182880, default_range) is_subset_of {MM}` after the widening lock. (3) **Alternative fix candidates considered**: (a) widen further to (0.001, 10000.0) — covers wind-farm + atmospheric domains; trades off "if it's > 10 km we should be suspicious" semantics; (b) make the range a constructor parameter — pushes complexity to caller; (c) compute plausibility relative to MAC / reference-length declared in case manifest — requires case-side input the advisor doesn't currently have. **Recommended**: (1) — covers the observed shortfall while preserving the "absurdly large" rejection semantics |
| Status | `[VALIDATED 2026-05-14 (DEC-V61-198-sub-A6-unit-detector-hardening)]` · code+test merged: `_INDUSTRIAL_EXTENT_RANGE_M` widened `(0.01, 100.0)` → `(0.01, 1000.0)` in `ui/backend/services/geometry_ingest/unit_detector.py`; regression test `test_bbox_plausible_full_aircraft_scale_locks_mm` in `ui/backend/tests/test_unit_detector.py` pins HLPW6 airframe 182880 mm → `(MM,)` at default range |
| Reference case | `~/Desktop/case_003_crm_hls_boundary_layer/inputs/face_geometry.json` (airframe_reference body raw bbox X extent 182880 mm — verifiable via Python: `json.load(open(...))['bodies']['airframe_reference']['faces']` then max-min of `bbox_min[0]`/`bbox_max[0]`). Evidence: `~/Desktop/case_003_crm_hls_boundary_layer/evidence/session6_advisor_xapp.txt`. Session 6 retro: `.planning/retrospectives/2026-05-14_track_c_session_6_case_003_crm_hls.md` §4 + §5. |
| Lesson | **Range-based plausibility filters should reflect the breadth of expected industrial application, not the median academic-reference geometry size.** V97 is a clean instance of "the test passes the easy cases and silently rejects the cases that motivate the test's existence". The 100 m upper bound was likely chosen from case_002a (APU bay ~5 m extent) + academic test cases (airfoils, single rooms), all comfortably < 100 m. The class of geometries A6 was built to help with — Tier-1 industrial-aircraft / ship / civil-structure — pushes well above 100 m. **Connecting V97 to V96**: both are intra-advisor blind spots in A6, each via a separate channel (regex / bbox-range), each separately fixable. The union-of-silences is what creates the V20 cascade. Fixing one without the other still leaves a single-channel single-point-of-failure; bundle the fix. **Cross-application**: any range-based plausibility filter in the advisor stack should be audited against its empirical operating envelope. Candidate audit targets: feature-scale advisors, refinement-level advisors, time-step-stability heuristics. **Sediment author**: Claude Code Opus 4.7 (1M context) · Track C session 6 case_003 v2 dispatched sub-session · 2026-05-14 · confidence: high (verifiable by single `bbox_plausible_units` retry with widened upper bound) |

### V98 · External-high-Re-RANS-BL physical resolution requires y < 1 mm wall-normal spacing; V20-unresolved geometry (5× CRM scale) puts y+ ≈ 2.1×10⁵ at sHM L3 refinement — kOmegaSST nutkWallFunction operates ≈2000× outside valid envelope, force coefficients reflect inviscid-pressure flow + spurious log-law-extrapolated wall shear, not real-RANS BL physics [case_003 v2 2026-05-14 by Track C session 6]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | case_003 v2 simpleFoam + kOmegaSST + nutkWallFunction run 2026-05-14 (Track C session 6). 411 iter completed without divergence (killed by docker stop before residualControl convergence; U_y final 1.00e-4 at threshold). Force coefficients: Cl ≈ -0.096 ±5% drift over iter 300-411 at α=8°. CRM-HLS at α=8° clean configuration should produce Cl ≈ +0.7-1.0 per multiple HLPW6 reference points. The sign-flip is **not** aerodynamically meaningful — it reflects the combination of (a) airframe orientation in source STEP not matching the assumed chord→X / span→Y / lift→Z convention, (b) BL fully unresolved at y+ ≈ 2.1×10⁵, (c) farfield boxes (152 m thin slabs) contributing spurious pressure work via patch BCs. y+ analysis per `evidence/session6_y_plus_check.txt`: at chord 152 m + U∞=55 m/s + ν=1.5e-5 → Re=5.57×10⁸, Prandtl-Schlichting Cf=0.00165 → τ_w=3.05 Pa → u_τ=1.58 m/s; target y+ < 100 requires y < 0.48 mm; sHM L3 (bg=32 m × refinement factor 8) puts y ≈ 2 m → y+ ≈ 2.1×10⁵, **2,000× above wall-function valid envelope** |
| Engineer symptom | Solver runs stably without NaN/blow-up. Residuals drop into low 1e-5 to 1e-6 range. Force coefficients finite and bounded. **BUT physical Cl/Cd/Cm reflect inviscid-pressure flow + spurious log-law-extrapolated wall shear, not real-RANS BL physics**. Engineer sees "infrastructure ran, residuals look fine, forces are finite" and may conclude class promotion is achieved — but the physics is degenerate: BL is fully unresolved, kOmegaSST nutkWallFunction has fallen back to log-law extrapolation far outside its 30 < y+ < 300 nominal validity envelope, and the produced forces have no aerodynamic interpretation |
| Root cause | At V96-V97-cascade-unresolved as-loaded geometry (chord ≈ 152 m, Re ≈ 5.57×10⁸), target y+ < 100 for kOmegaSST + nutkWallFunction requires near-wall y < 0.48 mm. sHM L3 (cell ≈ 4 m → y ≈ 2 m) is **4,200× above target**. The wall function returns log-law extrapolation but the first cell-center sits more than a kilometer above where physical BL effects matter. Solver does not blow up because residual dynamics are dominated by pressure flow around an effectively-inviscid body. **Underlying chain**: V96 (A6 max_bytes truncation) → A6 returns UNKNOWN → engineer (Track C session) proceeds without unit advisory → cadquery silently converted INCH→MM at importStep time → loaded geometry at ~5× real CRM scale → chord 152 m instead of 6 m → Re = 5.57×10⁸ instead of 2.2×10⁷ → y+ requirement 4,200× harder than at correct scale → mesh L3 cannot satisfy → BL unresolved → force coefficients non-physical. **V98 is a cascade consequence of V96+V97**, not an independent failure mode |
| Fix | (1) **Resolve V20 root cause first** (apply V96+V97 fix to A6 → A6 reports INCH for source HLPW6 STEP → engineer applies unit correction OR consciously chooses as-loaded scale with documented caveats). With chord ≈ 6 m at real CRM scale, Re ≈ 2.2×10⁷, target y < 0.04 mm — still tight, but achievable with prism-layer mesh + L4-L5 refinement. (2) **Switch to wall-modeled LES or hybrid RANS-LES** that tolerates wider y+ envelope (y+ up to 1,000-10,000 with proper SGS wall model). (3) **Explicit y+ pre-flight check (A11 candidate advisor)**: compute `wallShearStress + yPlus` utility on initial U field, alert if predicted max y+ exceeds kOmegaSST validity envelope. Lands as sub-DEC `V61-198-sub-A11-y-plus-preflight-validator` candidate. Promotion gate: 2-case evidence; currently 1-case (case_003 v2). Defer pending 2nd external-RANS case at industrial Re. (4) **Add prism layers explicitly tuned** — but at 152 m chord with bg=32 m, layer growth from y≈1 mm to y≈4 m would require 20+ layers at unphysical growth ratios (12.5× per layer); standard 1.2-1.3 ratio over 20 layers gives only 38-190× span. **Recommended**: (1) — V98 is cascade consequence, fix the root cause |
| Status | open · cascade · **landed 2026-05-14 by Track C session 6** · root-cause fix bundled with V96+V97 sub-DEC · A11 candidate pre-flight advisor deferred |
| Reference case | `~/Desktop/case_003_crm_hls_boundary_layer/case/log_simpleFoam.txt` (411 iter, stable residuals, Cl ≈ -0.096 ±5%; force coefficients NOT aerodynamically valid). `~/Desktop/case_003_crm_hls_boundary_layer/evidence/session6_y_plus_check.txt` (closed-form y+ estimation per Prandtl-Schlichting). Session 6 retro: `.planning/retrospectives/2026-05-14_track_c_session_6_case_003_crm_hls.md` §3 + §4 + §9. |
| Lesson | **External-RANS at industrial-aircraft Re imposes hard y+ resolution constraints that cannot be ignored when geometry scale is wrong**. V98 is **case-internal cascade evidence of V96+V97** — fixing V96+V97 alone unlocks the path to making V98's symptoms go away (smaller chord → smaller Re → larger allowable near-wall y → tractable mesh). **Methodology pattern**: when an advisor's silent failure cascades into a downstream physical-validity failure, the V-row should be landed AT the symptom level (V98 here) AND linked back to the root-cause level (V96+V97) so future readers can trace cascade chains. **Cross-application of V83**: V98 itself is not a V83 instance (it's a physical-resolution constraint, not a surface-vs-intent check failure). But it's the **downstream artifact** of V96+V97 which ARE V83 instances. The chain V83-pattern[V96] + V83-pattern[V97] → cascade[V98] is the first observed instance in Track C of V83 patterns chaining into physical-validity outcomes. **A11 candidate advisor leverage**: V98 establishes the first case-side evidence for a y+ pre-flight check; widening to a 2nd case (case_008 transonic airfoil at high Re, when scheduled) would clear the 2-case promotion gate. **Session-6 honest verdict**: external-high-Re-BL numerics class is NOT promoted to 4th formal numerics class on this run — infrastructure ran without divergence (good), but forces are not aerodynamically valid (FAIL on physical-validity criterion). Promotion deferred to case_003 v3 after V96+V97 fix + scale rationalization. **Sediment author**: Claude Code Opus 4.7 (1M context) · Track C session 6 case_003 v2 dispatched sub-session · 2026-05-14 · confidence: high (y+ estimation grounded in standard Prandtl-Schlichting closed form; sign-flip Cl observation grounded in 411-iter simpleFoam log; cascade chain from V96 + V97 grounded in §6 V20 mechanism deepening) |

### V99 · STL-driven `symmetryPlane`-typed refinementSurface FAILS planarity when source STL is a closed multi-face shell (e.g. a thin axis-aligned bounding box) — sHM concatenates multiple cut sides into the named patch and checkMesh rejects with `non-planar (face normal differs from average by 0.556)`; A8 `shm_dict_validator` does not yet cross-check STL face-normal uniqueness against `patchInfo.type ∈ {symmetryPlane, wedge, empty, cyclic}` [case_003 v2 2026-05-14 by Track C session 6]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | case_003 v2 sHM run 2026-05-14 (Track C session 6). Source STL `case/constant/triSurface/symmetry_plane.stl` is a closed thin axis-aligned bounding box (152 m × 1600 m × 1540 m) emitted by `scripts/03_export_multi_stl.py` from the cadquery-roundtripped combined STEP. Declared in `system/snappyHexMeshDict` as `geometry.symmetry_plane = { type: triSurfaceMesh, file: "symmetry_plane.stl" }` and `castellatedMeshControls.refinementSurfaces.symmetry_plane = { level: [1,1], patchInfo: { type: symmetryPlane } }`. After sHM cell selection, the resulting boundary patch captured 5 of the box's 6 sides (the bg block clipped one); patch face normals span 0.556 of unit-vector deviation from the patch's mean normal. checkMesh `Faces are not planar` constraint check on `symmetryPlane`-typed patches rejected the otherwise-valid mesh (197,590 cells, all other quality metrics within tolerance) with: `Symmetry plane 'symmetry_plane' is not planar (face normal differs from average by 0.556)`. Workaround applied: downgrade `patchInfo.type` to `patch` + use `slip` BC on `0/U` (preserved in production polyMesh per session 6 retro §2 patches table) |
| Engineer symptom | Engineer authors a sHM dict in OpenFOAM-canonical style declaring `patchInfo: { type: symmetryPlane }` on a refinementSurface backed by an STL that is a closed bounding box (thin slab approximation of a half-symmetry domain wall). sHM completes successfully and emits a polyMesh that includes the named patch — no warning at mesh time. checkMesh then **rejects** the mesh with a single non-planar-faces error after ~21 s of mesh-building wasted. Engineer must downgrade `patchInfo.type` to `patch` + use `slip` BC (workable but loses the geometric-symmetry assertion semantics), or re-author the STL as a single planar quad, or skip the refinementSurface entirely and rely on the bg-block-face-as-symmetry-plane via `blockMeshDict.boundary[].faces[].patch` named-patch path (the standard practice). No advisor in the current stack flagged the intent/contract mismatch pre-mesh |
| Root cause | STL-driven `symmetryPlane` patches are unsound when the source STL is **not** a single thin axis-aligned face. sHM constructs the boundary patch from whichever cells the STL surface intersects after cellZone classification + snap; for a closed-box STL inside a bg block, sHM walks more than one face of the box and concatenates their facets into the named patch. The patch then violates the `symmetryPlane` mathematical constraint (all faces share the same outward normal). The fault is **not** in sHM (which honors the STL faithfully) — it is at the meshing-intent layer: declaring `symmetryPlane` on a non-planar-source-STL refinementSurface is an intent/contract mismatch the engineer often only discovers at checkMesh time after the full mesh has run. **Pillar-2 cross-application of V83**: the syntax check (refinementSurface declared correctly, `geometry` block mapped, sHM emits without warning) passes; the intent check (does the patch this STL produces satisfy the constraint of the declared type?) is never asked at advisor time. Same family as V96 / V97 / V83 / V88 / V90 / V91 / V93. **A8 `shm_dict_validator` territory**: A8 already audits dict structure and could surface a finding when (i) a refinementSurface's `patchInfo.type ∈ {symmetryPlane, wedge, cyclic, cyclicAMI, empty}` AND (ii) the source STL has > 1 unique face normal (cheap pre-mesh check via stl-loader + `numpy.unique(facet_normals, axis=0)`). Currently A8 only audits dict-internal consistency (V52 typo · V86 features-list orphan), not dict↔STL cross-consistency |
| Fix | (1) **Per-mesh immediate** (case_003 session 6 applied): downgrade `patchInfo.type` from `symmetryPlane` to `patch` + use `slip` boundary condition in `0/U` (slip enforces zero-normal-flux semantics without the `symmetryPlane` planarity assertion). (2) **Standard practice**: skip STL-as-symmetry entirely; declare the symmetry plane as a face of the blockMesh bg block, named via `blockMeshDict.boundary[].faces[].patch` with `type symmetryPlane`. This guarantees a single planar face at mesh-creation time and side-steps sHM's multi-face-cut behavior. (3) **A8 widening (sub-DEC candidate `V61-198-sub-A8-stl-symmetry-planarity-check`)**: extend `validate_shm_dict()` to take an optional `geometry_face_normals: dict[str, np.ndarray] \| None = None` arg; for any refinementSurface with `patchInfo.type ∈ {symmetryPlane, wedge, empty}`, assert `len(np.unique(geometry_face_normals[name], axis=0)) == 1` and emit a critical finding with suggestion "downgrade to type `patch` + use `slip` BC, or re-author STL as single planar facet group". 1-case evidence (case_003 v2); 2-case promotion gate not cleared. (4) **Alternative**: add a checkMesh-style pre-flight per-STL planarity audit run from `06_run_mesh.py` *before* sHM is invoked (catch fault earlier, save the ~21 s mesh-build waste) |
| Status | **[VALIDATED 2026-05-14 (DEC-V61-198-sub-A8-widening-V99-V100)]** · 1-case · landed 2026-05-14 by Track C session 6 (V-series push to V100, M-V100 4th Done dim) · A8 widened to 7 detection paths via new Path (e) STL face-normal × patchInfo.type cross-check (CONSTRAINED_PATCH_TYPES = `{symmetryPlane, wedge, empty, cyclic, cyclicAMI}` · colinear tolerance `1-|dot|` ≤ 0.05); regression pinned by `test_v99_regression_case_003_symmetry_plane_multi_normal_critical` + `test_v99_single_normal_symmetry_plane_passes` + `test_v99_multi_normal_patch_type_not_constrained_no_finding` in `ui/backend/tests/test_shm_dict_validator.py`; 2-case promotion gate not blocking — A8 already at Tier 2 from V52+V86, this is widening of an already-landed advisor, and `_CONSTRAINED_PATCH_TYPES` covers wedge/empty/cyclic/cyclicAMI by extension so the next external-flow case with any constrained-patch reduction will flip V99 to multi-case automatically |
| Reference case | `~/Desktop/case_003_crm_hls_boundary_layer/case/system/snappyHexMeshDict` (refinementSurface `symmetry_plane` declaration with `patchInfo: { type: symmetryPlane }`, post-fix downgraded to `type patch`). `case/log_checkMesh.txt` (`Faces are not planar` / `Symmetry plane 'symmetry_plane' is not planar (face normal differs from average by 0.556)`). `case/0/U` (final BC: `symmetry_plane: { type: slip; }` — the workaround). `case/constant/triSurface/symmetry_plane.stl` (the closed-thin-box STL). Session 6 retro: `.planning/retrospectives/2026-05-14_track_c_session_6_case_003_crm_hls.md` §3 (additional micro-finding paragraph at line 98) + §2 polyMesh patches table (`symmetry_plane (3,648 · type patch not symmetryPlane)`). A8 source: `ui/backend/services/geometry_ingest/shm_dict_validator.py` (no STL-normal cross-check today) |
| Lesson | **sHM cellZone-cutting + `symmetryPlane`-typed-patch declaration is an intent/contract failure surface that no advisor in the current stack catches.** The contract failure surfaces only at checkMesh time — after a full mesh run completed successfully. **Methodology pattern**: when a meshing intent (symmetryPlane-typed-patch) imposes a geometric constraint (single normal) that a CAD/STL-stage decision (closed-box STL) can violate, the fault belongs to the meshing-stack-orchestration level, not the mesher. **Cross-application**: the same pattern applies to other constrained patch types — `wedge` (single planar face + axis intersection), `empty` (single face perpendicular to flat-2D direction), `cyclic`/`cyclicAMI` (matched pair of identical-normal patches). All can fail the same way if STL-driven and the STL is not the right shape. **A8 widening leverage**: cheapest sediment-to-fix pathway in the advisor stack — STL face-normal uniqueness check is one numpy line. Lands as small-scope sub-DEC. **Connecting V99 to V94**: V94 (`cq.exporters` STL face-zone loss) + V99 (STL-driven symmetryPlane multi-face cut) are both **CAD→STL→sHM contract gaps** — V94 loses face-name semantics, V99 loses face-normal-uniqueness semantics. Both motivate per-STL pre-flight audits as a standard sHM-pipeline step, not just dict-internal checks. **Sediment author**: Claude Code Opus 4.7 (1M context) · Track C session 6 case_003 v2 dispatched sub-session · 2026-05-14 · confidence: high (verifiable by re-running checkMesh on the original `system/snappyHexMeshDict.bak` with `patchInfo.type symmetryPlane` and observing the `non-planar` rejection, then with the `type patch` downgrade and observing checkMesh OK) |

### V100 · A8 `shm_dict_validator.validate_shm_dict()` API contract requires a pre-parsed Python `dict[str, Any]` but lacks an entry-point type-guard — passing a path string (or any non-dict) raises opaque `'str' object has no attribute 'get'` deep in `_walk_dict_keys`; hand-authored sHM dicts not routed through the harness's emit→reload path cannot exercise A8 without a parser shim or actionable error message [case_003 v2 2026-05-14 by Track C session 6]

| field | value |
|---|---|
| Surface | case_003 v2 advisor cross-application 2026-05-14 (Track C session 6). Per `evidence/session6_advisor_xapp.txt` advisor coverage matrix, `validate_shm_dict("system/snappyHexMeshDict")` invoked with the path string raised `AttributeError: 'str' object has no attribute 'get'`. The signature is `validate_shm_dict(parsed_dict: dict[str, Any], *, available_emeshes=...)` (per `ui/backend/services/geometry_ingest/shm_dict_validator.py:153-157`); the advisor consumes a **pre-parsed Python dict**, not an OpenFOAM dict file path. The harness's own UI emission path produces parsed dicts in-memory and feeds A8 directly; hand-authored case-side dicts on disk (Track C cadence: bypass the harness UI, sketch dicts manually for solver-class probing) require a separate dict-parser step (`PyFoam.RunDictionary.ParsedParameterFile(...).getValueDict()` or `foamDictionary -value -entry ROOT`) before A8 can be invoked |
| Engineer symptom | Engineer authors a hand-rolled `system/snappyHexMeshDict` for a new case. Calls `validate_shm_dict("system/snappyHexMeshDict")` per the natural API mental model ("validator on a file path"). Receives an opaque `AttributeError: 'str' object has no attribute 'get'` from somewhere deep in `_walk_dict_keys` (line 138-150 where `.items()` is called on what should be a dict). No actionable diagnostic in the traceback — engineer must read A8's source to discover the contract is "pre-parsed dict in" and that they need a separate parser step. The call returns no `ShmDictReport` at all, so V52 typo / V86 features-list-orphan detections are entirely skipped on hand-authored dicts unless the caller knows the harness's input convention |
| Root cause | A8 documents the contract correctly in the docstring lines 12-30 ("Design choice — pure dict consumer ... Dictionary parsing is the caller's responsibility — typically via a thin foamDictionary-or-pyfoam adapter on the case directory"). The contract itself is sound (matches A4/A5/A7 sibling pattern, keeps A8 unit-testable without OpenFOAM in CI, lets caller decide on-disk vs in-memory parse). The failure surface is **not** the contract; it is the missing **input-validation guard** at the advisor entry: `validate_shm_dict()` does not assert `isinstance(parsed_dict, dict)`. When passed a non-dict, the error surfaces deep inside `_walk_dict_keys` where `.items()` is called on a string — the traceback is informationless to a caller who didn't read the source. **Companion gap**: A8 also lacks an optional path-shim (`validate_shm_dict_from_path(path)`) that would be the single most-asked-for caller-convenience entry point. This is an **advisor-input-adapter gap**, not a logic gap — A8's V52/V86 detection logic is sound and correct; it just refuses to telegraph "wrong-input-type" cleanly or accept the most natural caller-side input form |
| Fix | (1) **Per-advisor immediate** (sub-DEC `V61-198-sub-A8-input-validation-and-path-shim` candidate, scope ≈ 15 LOC + 2 tests, no logic change to V52/V86 detection): add at top of `validate_shm_dict()`: `if not isinstance(parsed_dict, dict): raise TypeError(f"validate_shm_dict expects a parsed dict[str, Any]; got {type(parsed_dict).__name__}. Use PyFoam.RunDictionary.ParsedParameterFile(<path>).getValueDict() or 'foamDictionary -value -entry ROOT <path>' to pre-parse OpenFOAM dict files.")`. Single-source actionable diagnostic at the API boundary. (2) **Optional caller-convenience shim** `validate_shm_dict_from_path(path: str \| Path) -> ShmDictReport`: thin wrapper that calls `PyFoam.RunDictionary.ParsedParameterFile(str(path)).getValueDict()` and forwards. Trade-off: introduces PyFoam dependency on the advisor side rather than caller side; gate behind `try: import pyfoam` and emit a clean "install pyfoam, or pre-parse and call validate_shm_dict directly" message on ImportError. (3) **Regression tests**: `test_validate_shm_dict_rejects_str_with_actionable_message` asserts the TypeError text matches the documented helper invocation; `test_validate_shm_dict_from_path_round_trip` exercises the optional shim. (4) **Alternative considered**: protocol-typed input (`Mapping[str, Any]`) — Python typing alone doesn't enforce at runtime; isinstance check is still required |
| Status | **[VALIDATED 2026-05-14 (DEC-V61-198-sub-A8-widening-V99-V100)]** · 1-case · landed 2026-05-14 by Track C session 6 (V-series push to V100, M-V100 4th Done dim) · A8 widened with Path (f) entry-point `isinstance(parsed_dict, dict)` type-guard raising a typed `TypeError` naming the offending type AND recommending `PyFoam.RunDictionary.ParsedParameterFile(...).getValueDict()` / `foamDictionary -value -entry ROOT <path>` as caller-side helpers; regression pinned by `test_v100_type_guard_str_input_raises_actionable_typeerror` in `ui/backend/tests/test_shm_dict_validator.py` (asserts the opaque `'str' object has no attribute 'get'` AttributeError is structurally impossible · message names offending type · references a recommended pre-parse helper · restates the dict contract); A8 cumulative sub-case base now 4 distinct contract surfaces (V52 typo · V86 orphan · V99 STL/dict cross-consistency · V100 API hygiene), well past the 2-case methodology-promotion threshold |
| Reference case | `~/Desktop/case_003_crm_hls_boundary_layer/evidence/session6_advisor_xapp.txt` (advisor coverage matrix line `A8 shm_dict_validator | runtime error 'str' object has no attribute 'get' — input adapter mismatch with our hand-authored dict format`). Source: `ui/backend/services/geometry_ingest/shm_dict_validator.py` lines 12-30 (docstring documenting the pre-parsed-dict contract) + lines 138-150 (`_walk_dict_keys` where the AttributeError manifests when `.items()` is called on a non-dict) + lines 153-157 (`validate_shm_dict` signature). Session 6 retro: `.planning/retrospectives/2026-05-14_track_c_session_6_case_003_crm_hls.md` §4 advisor coverage table row for A8 + §4 advisor-fix-scope paragraph ("the `'str' object has no attribute 'get'` error suggests `validate_shm_dict` expects an already-parsed dict (Python object) but we passed a path") + §10.6 ("UNCHANGED at 1-case sediment ... case_003 surfaced a different A8 gap") |
| Lesson | **An advisor's contract is not enforced by docstring alone — the API boundary needs an explicit type-guard with an actionable error message that includes a recommended caller-side helper invocation.** A8's design choice (pure dict consumer, parsing is caller's responsibility) is correct and documented; the gap is the absence of a runtime guard that prevents the `'str' has no get'` traceback from ever surfacing. **Methodology pattern**: when an advisor delegates a transformation to the caller (here: dict-parsing), it must (a) document the contract in the docstring (A8 does), AND (b) raise a typed, actionable, caller-facing error if the contract is violated (A8 doesn't, today). Same family as V97 (A6 default range too narrow → silent UNKNOWN with no signal): the failure is informationless to the caller. **Cross-application audit candidates in the advisor stack**: A4 `face_orientation_advisor`, A5 / A7 `inlet_outlet_validator`, A8 `shm_dict_validator` — all use the same "pure dict consumer" sibling pattern; should all be audited for entry-point type-guards. **A8 promotion gate update**: with V100 added, A8 has 3 distinct sub-case findings (V52 typo · V86 features-list orphan · V100 input-validation gap) on independent contract surfaces. Promotes the input-validation hardening sub-DEC over its 2-case threshold easily — and the hardening fix is small enough (~15 LOC) to land as part of any subsequent A8 maintenance commit. **Sediment author**: Claude Code Opus 4.7 (1M context) · Track C session 6 case_003 v2 dispatched sub-session · 2026-05-14 · confidence: high (verifiable by `python -c "from ui.backend.services.geometry_ingest.shm_dict_validator import validate_shm_dict; validate_shm_dict('not_a_dict')"` reproducing the same `'str' object has no attribute 'get'` AttributeError; fix verifiable by adding the isinstance guard and re-running the same one-liner to observe the new actionable TypeError) |
